## Gold's quiet hours (Oct 29, 2026, the owner: "gold skip can go live")

`QUIET_HOURS_CT = { KXGOLD15M: [20, 24] }` in `functions/kalshiLiveLib.js`: gold places no NEW order while the US Central clock reads 20:00 to 23:59 (by the time the bot looks; follows daylight saving). Why: MKTTIME1 (V2, 2026-10-29) found this block the one place gold was weaker in all three history windows (PF 1.44 / 1.03 / 1.17 become 1.63 / 1.16 / 1.28 without it, 24 losses skipped in 315 entries) and the live block was slightly net negative on a small sample. It is a test, not a finding. **Corrected the same evening (V2's falsifier FALS1):** the first z of +0.98 tested the wrong thing; on gold's own close-date windows the middle window lifts profit factor only +0.085 (gate 0.10), the block's permutation p is 0.025 alone and about 0.12 to 0.30 counting every market and block tried, and the corrected block-versus-rest z is 1.87 (about 3c an entry worse). The owner kept it live anyway ("keep it, review sooner"). **Interim review:** MKTTIME1-F reads it early at 100 block entries on fresh days from public candles (about Nov 24 to 26; the live bot places none in the block, so there are no live block orders to read). If the block's mean per entry is not below the rest (z under 0), delete the table entry; if it is below by z 1.5 or more, keep it; between the two, wait for the December read. It only removes orders; the exit watch still sells an open gold position. The formal MKTTIME1-F read stays in about December. The Bot page's silent-market warning says nothing about gold during the block and restarts its clock at midnight. Tests QH1, QH2. Needs `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh`.

## Market News filter (Oct 29, 2026, the owner: "only pull equities, crypto or commodity related articles")

**(History: the news feeds and `isMarketNews` were removed from the tape on Nov 11, 2026, see "Market strip and Brief moved off Yahoo"; git history has the code.)** `isMarketNews` in `functions/marketTapeLib.js` ran on every news feed BEFORE the per feed cap: a headline is kept only when it names stocks, crypto or commodities, or the Fed, rates, inflation, yields, dollar or tariff news that moves them (`NEWS_ALLOW`), and a personal advice column or lifestyle piece is dropped even when a market word appears in it (`NEWS_DENY`: first-person starts, "my husband", Moneyist, "should I", horoscopes and the like). The two crypto desks (CoinDesk, Cointelegraph) pass on their source, but the drop list still applies. The trigger was a MarketWatch advice column about an ex-husband and a college fund. If every story in a run is filtered out, no news is written and the page keeps the last headlines. Tests NF1, NF2 (and T3 now uses a market headline). The first list was run against the live feeds the same evening and widened (price-move verbs such as "tanks" and "plunges", "free cash flow", "capex", jobless claims, and about thirty large company names) because it dropped real equity stories such as "Alphabet tanks nearly 13% for worst day ever"; of 70 live headlines it now drops 19, all politics, world news and advice columns. **Needs** `npx firebase-tools deploy --only functions:marketTapeTick --project shwoopnet` (keyless, no switch); the page changes nothing and the stored headlines refresh at the next 15 minute run after the deploy. To widen or narrow it, edit the two word lists and the tests together.

## The freshness check (Oct 29, 2026, the owner: "check for more stale modules or information ... an agent that runs after every deploy")

The Bot page's Rules and limits text, the Start confirmation, the next look label and the silent-market list say things the bot's code decides (which markets trade and which are paused, sizes, the window limit, the loss stop, the exit line, the add-on cap, gold's quiet hours). They went stale twice in one day (XRP's "10 minute look", Dogecoin's 3 contracts, Bitcoin and the probes still listed). `test/siteFreshnessGates.test.js` (SF1 to SF5) now compares each of those lines with `PAUSED_SERIES`, `L1_SERIES`, `FIXED_CONTRACTS` and the limit constants, so a code change that makes the page untrue fails in the pull request that makes it. **When you change a size, a limit, the paused list or the gold quiet hours, change the page text and this test together.** Also run: `.claude/agents/freshness.md` (read only: it runs the gate, then reads the page's visible text, the changelog and the docs for lines the gate cannot pin, and reports with proof; it never edits or deploys). `functions/deploy-sizing.sh` and `functions/deploy-push.sh` run the gate at the end of a deploy and warn, never block. The gate also runs on every pull request through the Release Manager, and the agent runs weekly (Sundays), in a fresh session, as a deep read of the page text and docs the gate cannot pin (it was daily until Nov 7, 2026).

## The nightly order report (Oct 28, 2026, the owner: "reports at midnight (00:00 UTC)")

`kalshiNightlyReport` (`functions/kalshiReportLib.js`, `functions/deploy-report.sh`, `test/kalshiReportGates.test.js` R1 to R5) is a READ-ONLY HTTPS endpoint behind one secret token (`KALSHI_REPORT_TOKEN`, Secret Manager, at least 24 characters, compared in constant time; no token configured means closed). It returns a summary of the bot's own orders for the last N hours (default 24, at most 90 days): filled, settled, won, lost, exits, open, net, profit factor, worst loss, split by market and by entry lead, and with `?orders=1` one compact row per order. It never returns an order id, a client order id or a credential. It holds no Kalshi key, sends nothing to Kalshi, writes nothing, and only reads `kalshiLiveOrders`. It is the ONE reviewed exception to the security scan's "no open HTTP endpoint" rule (`scan.js` allows it only with `makeReportHandler` and the report token). The token goes into the Claude environment's secrets, never into a chat; a scheduled task calls it with `Authorization: Bearer <token>`. **Schedule and dates (Oct 29, 2026):** the scheduled fetch now runs at 12:04 AM US Central and the relay at 12:13 AM US Central (they were 00:00 UTC). Report days are US Central days, and every time shown to the owner is US Central. The 7 day figures still include the paused markets (XRP and the probes) until their orders age out of the window. Needs `bash functions/deploy-report.sh` (it prints a new token once, in the owner's terminal, and keeps an existing one).

## Research outcomes, Oct 29, 2026 (facts only, nothing promoted)

From `kalshi-scalper/README.md` on the research branch. No trading rule changed because of any of it.

- **GEN1, GEN1S, GEN2:** generators of full scalp strategies on the 18 fifteen minute series. 17,500 distinct strategies in all (5,000 + 5,000 + 7,500), 0 passed discovery (profit factor 1.5 at 10 contracts, per-order rounded fees). The best discovery profit factor was 1.25 (GEN1, GEN1S) and 1.14 (GEN2, at least 150 trades). N tried for deflation is about 425,000 (425,330 after GEN2), Bonferroni z bar about 5.2 [superseded: the ledger now counts about 2.1 million tried, z 5.46 one-sided (`DEFLATION_LEDGER.md` on the `rnd-queue` branch)]. Holdouts not read, nothing frozen.
- **ARB2 to ARB5:** full-set, deadline, bracket and sports totals lock scans. 0 opportunities of 1c or more that were real locks (ARB2 to ARB4 best +1.4c, +0.6c, +0.1c; the +1.4c pair was not a lock; ARB5 zero crossed ladders in 691 games). All FALSIFIED.
- **THETA1:** favourite drift sold before settlement in long-dated markets. 0 of 27 cells survived discovery (profit factor 0.03 to 0.34).
- **EXIT1: FALSIFIED.** Holding to settlement beats every exit shape. Profit factor per contract in three windows: hold 0.961 / 0.928 / 0.898, live 25c line 0.921 / 0.897 / 0.869.
- **REGIME1:** all five stand-down filters FALSIFIED. One information-only effect: entries with a spread of 2c or more do worse (removed entries 0.798 / 0.753 / 0.716). Not a pass.
- **GOLD1D:** the gold stability claim fails. Gold at the 6 minute look 1.20, at the 10 minute look 0.89.
- **G3H1 (hourly ladder) pass is VOID.** An audit found a universe look-ahead: the ladder file kept only strikes whose YES mid was between 3c and 97c 35 to 25 minutes before the close, but the rule enters 51 to 46 minutes before, so cheap strikes that later collapsed were dropped from the source file. CORR1 re-reads it on a file with strikes chosen before entry.
- **Queued, not run as of this note:** CORR1, LADL1, CONF1, MM1.

**Standing lesson:** a universe or file built after the entry time can hide the losers. Choose the strikes (or markets) from information available before entry, and say how the file was built before trusting a result from it.

## Order size lock and exit watch alarm (Oct 29, 2026)

**Why.** On Oct 28 an order reached the account with no `entryLead` and a size the current code would not send for that market (`FIXED_CONTRACTS`; current code also tags every order). Nothing in code stopped a wrongly sized order. **Size lock.** `maxContractsFor(series)` (`functions/kalshiLiveLib.js`) is the most an L1 order for that series may have: the fixed size for XRP, Dogecoin and natural gas, 1 for the other capped and probe series, `L1_ORDER_CEILING` (50) for gold, the one ladder market, and 0 for a series not in `L1_SERIES`. `runL1Tick` checks it after all the sizing and window rules and BEFORE the order is recorded, so a refused count sends nothing, leaves no "sending" document, logs an `order size refused` event and the other markets carry on. The fixed sizes are copied once when the module loads, so a change in memory moves the sizing but not the lock; it reads code constants only (no session, argument, stored value or page input). It can only skip an order, never raise a count, and a valid order is unchanged. **What it cannot do:** it does not find out WHY a 12 came out (an old build running during a deploy, a manual order and a bug all look alike to it), a wrong edit to the constants moves the lock with them, and gold is bounded by the ceiling, not by its ladder. The nightly report (`kalshiReportLib.js`) has an `oversize` section (series, count, limit, time; zero is normal), which is also how a hand-placed or old-build order shows up. **Exit watch alarm.** `functions/kalshiExitAlarmLib.js`: the exit watch writes a heartbeat (`kalshiLiveControl/exitWatch`, once per minute's run, after a pass completed); the minute function `kalshiLiveArmed`, a different process, checks it when the bot is on and, if a position is open and the watch has been silent more than `EXIT_WATCH_STALE_MS` (4 minutes), writes `exitWatchAlert` on the session document once per silent spell (claimed in a transaction first); the existing `kalshiPushSession` turns that into an issue alert (`sessionKinds`, kind `exitwatch`). No new secret, endpoint or scheduled function, no Kalshi key, no order, every error swallowed. **What it cannot do:** it does not sell or restart anything, it cannot see a stall if BOTH scheduled functions are down (the dead-man's switch covers that), and an alert is lost if no phone is subscribed. Needs three deploys, IN THIS ORDER: `bash functions/deploy-push.sh` FIRST (heartbeat in `kalshiLiveExit`, alert text in `kalshiPushSession`), then `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh` (size lock and alarm check in `kalshiLiveArmed`), then `bash functions/deploy-report.sh` (the report's `oversize` section). If the alarm check goes out before the heartbeat exists, any position open more than 4 minutes looks silent and one false alert fires; it is harmless but noisy. Gates: SZ1 to SZ6 (`test/kalshiSizeCheckGates.test.js`), OV1 to OV3 (`test/kalshiOversizeReportGates.test.js`), EA1 to EA9 (`test/kalshiExitAlarmGates.test.js`).

## One deploy command (Oct 30, 2026, the owner: "build the wrapper")

`bash functions/deploy-latest.sh` updates the folder to `origin/main` (it stops if tracked files have local edits), runs the whole test suite, works out from the changed files which of `deploy-push.sh`, `deploy-sizing.sh` and `deploy-report.sh` are needed (in that order), shows the plan and asks before running anything. It only calls those three scripts, never pushes, forces or touches a secret, and records the last deployed commit in the gitignored `functions/.last-deployed` only after every selected deploy finished (a failed deploy is offered again next run). The first run has no marker and asks whether everything up to now is already deployed. `DRY_RUN=1` and `ASSUME_YES=1` exist for the test. Gates DL1 to DL4 (`test/deployLatestGates.test.js`). It does not replace the individual scripts, which still work alone.

## Profit add-on cap (Oct 29, 2026)

The owner: "i'm concerned about us losing more than we're making due to scaling". The reinvest pool buys one extra contract per $0.93 of pool with no limit but `L1_ORDER_CEILING` (50). Wins are about 6c a contract and losses about 90c, so through a win streak the add-on compounds and the next loss lands at peak size. `l1Count` now clamps the add-on to `addonCap(base)` = floor(base x `ADDON_MAX_FRACTION` 0.25), where base is the balance-driven count (base 23 allows at most +5, base 11 at most +2, base under 4 gets 0). It can only lower a count. The pool still accumulates beyond what is used, on purpose, so it reflects real profit and raising the fraction is a reviewed edit; `addonUsed` (and so `addonCost` and the loss stop's `addonAtRisk`) is the clamped count actually sent, so the unused pool is never counted as at risk. The session's `sizeAddon` is clamped by the cap-based bound and the page's pool line reads `sizePool` itself. Not changed: `SKIM_REINVEST`, `foldSkim`, the 12% stop, the window cap, the 25c exit, the ceilings, capped and probe series. Arithmetic (not predictions) at 6c a win and 90c a loss, largest order and largest single loss, before (pool unbounded) then after: base 11: 50 ($45.00) then 13 ($11.70); base 23: 50 ($45.00) then 28 ($25.20); base 40: 50 then 50 (the ceiling already binds). Tests `test/kalshiAddonCapGates.test.js`. Needs `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh`.

## Politics shadow recorder (Nov 11, 2026, the owner)

`.github/workflows/record-politics.yml`, `scripts/record_politics.py` and the committed list `kalshi-scalper/workers/POLREC_SERIES.json` (32 series at that date, at most 60, now 51; a list edit is a reviewed change) record Kalshi POLITICS markets forward. Why: the politics census found a thin universe (only two series with 30 or more settled events; most weekly series are 8 to 12 events old), so history has to accrue. **What is recorded:** every 30 minutes, for each open market of each listed series (one cursor-paged public read per series), the ticker, event, open and close time, yes and no bid and ask, bid and ask size, last price, volume, open interest and the server Date header (`quotes.csv`, so close time drift shows); once an hour the result of markets that closed in the last 48 hours (`results.csv`, read by ticker, a repeat across jobs is expected); once a day (the job at 00 UTC) `daily.csv`, per series: open markets, recorded, skipped by the 14 day cap, skipped for no readable close time. **The 14 day cap (the owner's hold limit):** a market is recorded only if it closes within 14 days of now AND its open-to-close span is at most 14 days (`within_cap`; no readable close time fails closed). Long-dated series (Fed chair, shutdown length, Hormuz normalisation and similar) stay out and are listed under `later_only` in the JSON; scheduled-event series with one long market are kept and the cap skips just that market, counted in the daily file. **Branch:** gzipped CSV on its OWN orphan branch `pol-data` under `polrec/<date>/<HHMM>/`, pushed with the built-in token (six tries), never main and never `quote-data` or `nc-data`. **Stop date:** 2027-02-28 23:59 UTC, checked in the workflow's first step and in the script; stop sooner by deleting the workflow. **It places nothing and holds no key:** public Kalshi market data only, one host, no auth header, no signing, no POST, redirects refused, a generic User-Agent, no Firestore, nothing under `functions/` or on the page. A 429 waits 5 s doubling to 60; a body that is not an object is no answer and never aborts the run; a hard per-run request budget (600) and at least 5 s between calls (at most 12 a minute). **Estimates (not measured yet):** an hourly start runs about 35 minutes and polls twice, about 32 requests a poll plus 32 for the results read and a few for paging, so about 1 request a minute on average; about 840 runner minutes a day, free on a public repo; a few MB a day gzipped. Gates PR1 to PR10 in `test/politicsRecorderGates.test.js`; `test/recorderPushRetryGates.test.js` knows the `pol-data` loop.

## Open-Meteo stopped, weather moves to the NWS API (Nov 11, 2026, the owner)

Open-Meteo's hosted API is free for non-commercial use only, and Shwoop Financials is an LLC trading real money, so every Open-Meteo read was stopped. **What stopped:** the ensemble call in `scripts/record_wxens.py` (the `ens` and `ens_low` tables) and the `ensemble-api.open-meteo.com` entry in the signal logger's host allowlist (`scripts/record_signals.py`, gate SG1 updated on purpose). **What replaced it:** the same recorder, workflow (`record-wxens.yml`), schedule, cities and `quote-data` branch (`wxens/`), now NWS only (`api.weather.gov`, US government, public domain, no key): the point forecast high and low it already read, plus new `nwsgrid` (gridpoint max, min and hourly temperature in Celsius) and `nwsobs` (latest station reading) tables, beside the Kalshi quotes, results and the IEM observed extremes. NWS has no ensemble product, so the WXENS1 ensemble test cannot gather new days; `kalshi-scalper/src/scalper/wxens1.py` still reads the old `ens` rows already on the branch, and any new design on NWS data needs its own registration. Climate reports were not added (IEM already carries the daily extremes). The recorder pins its hosts (NWS, Kalshi, IEM), refuses redirects, sends a generic User-Agent with no contact details, and records nothing after 2027-02-28. Cost: a run is about one billed minute, two starts an hour, about 1,440 runner minutes a month, as before (the repo is public). **Gates:** `test/noOpenMeteoGates.test.js` OM1 to OM3 (no host in workflows, scripts, functions, the allowlist or the registry), `test/wxensRecorderGates.test.js` W9, W10. Data already collected stays.

## Bitcoin and Ethereum: smooth climb only (Oct 23, 2026)

The research session's SMOOTH2 lead, the owner: "1" (confirmed in this session). Bitcoin and Ethereum (`SMOOTH_SERIES`) both decide at the 6 minute look (Bitcoin left the 10 minute list) and place their usual one contract order ONLY IF the bought side's 1 minute mid path from the market's first candle to the decision candle has at least 4 earlier candles, every minute change at or above -0.005 and a total climb of at least 0.10 (`smoothGate`, `pathShape`; plain float arithmetic, so a pullback of exactly 0.005 is NOT smooth, as in the research session's parity vectors, test SM1). A path that cannot be read, is stale or is not smooth places no order and says so in the note. The gate reads public candles only (no key) and can only REMOVE Bitcoin and Ethereum orders; size, band, stops, caps, the 25c exit and the one crypto order per window are unchanged. Every order also records `minStep`, `climb` and `smooth`. Note the list order still puts Bitcoin before Dogecoin at the 6 minute look, so a smooth Bitcoin takes the window from Dogecoin's 3 contracts. **Kill rules (README, registered before any order):** remove Bitcoin and Ethereum from live orders if 3 or more of the first 40 smooth fills lose, or their combined net is under $0 after 100 fills, or the 2026-11-12 forward read is FALSIFIED; review at the 2026-11-17 export. Tests SM1 to SM3. Needs `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh`.

## Money across Kalshi's shards: topped up by hand (Oct 24, 2026)

Kalshi keeps each market group's money on its own exchange shard (CORRECTED Oct 30, 2026: the owner's orders export shows gold, Dogecoin and Bitcoin on shard 2, so every live market is on shard 2; the old line that put gold on shard 0 was stale and once led to wrong advice. Only AUD/USD and USD/CAD were on shard 0 and they are off. Trust the order's own `exchangeIndex`, not this note) and an order needs money on ITS shard, so a shard that ran short silently stopped a market (the forex probes sat unfunded for days). An automatic split was built on Oct 23, 2026 (Kalshi's opt-in auto-rebalance target, `kalshiShardSplit`) and **removed the next day**: Kalshi answered the key's request with HTTP 403 (the bot's key may not set that target), and the web "Advanced shard settings" page has no such setting, only the "Disable balance management" switch (leave it off) and the per-exchange balances. So the owner tops a shard up by hand, Kalshi, Transfers. The page cannot move money. It says so early instead: the Bot page's account card adds "Shard N (...) is low. Top it up in Kalshi under Transfers." and the page said so while a bot shard held under $30 (removed Oct 24, 2026). The raw account read still lists the balance per shard. Delete the deployed function with `firebase functions:delete kalshiShardSplit --project shwoopnet` (nothing calls it).

## One window, one limit

*Superseded, see "State as of Nov 13, 2026" near the top; kept as the record of what was decided then.*

Bitcoin, gold and Ethereum close in the same 15 minutes and move together, so `runL1Tick` holds the account money put into one window (matched by the ticker's time part, counting earlier ticks) to `L1_WINDOW_CAP_FRACTION` (5.5%, raised from 4.5% on Oct 20, 2026 so the last series stops getting 1 contract) of the sizing base, under the loss stop (`L1_SIZED_STOP_FRACTION`, 6% from Oct 20, 2026, **then 12% the same night**: at 6% a full open window (counted as lost) plus ordinary settled losses ended the bot, because the window limit left only a small room under the stop; one ordinary loss was enough. Open orders are still counted as lost, so 12% of the base is the real worst case; tests W4 and W5 pin both numbers). Series are sent in `L1_SERIES` order (Bitcoin, gold, Ethereum, XRP, Dogecoin), so the later ones are the ones that go without. The add-on (profit) is not counted; the first order in a window is never cut to zero. Tests W1 to W3 in `test/kalshiL1Gates.test.js`.

## One crypto order per window (Oct 22, 2026)

*Superseded, see "State as of Nov 13, 2026" near the top; kept as the record of what was decided then.*

Bitcoin, Ethereum, XRP and Dogecoin (`CAPPED_SERIES`) now place at most ONE order per 15 minute window (`runL1Tick`, first in `L1_SERIES` order wins; an order that did not fill does not hold the window). Why: the research session's live diagnosis found 72% of recent loss dollars came from three windows where two of them lost together. Gold and the probes are not part of the rule. History is in-sample and the rule roughly halves the expected crypto profit, so it is a test: read it on the live record, not the backtest. Test OC1. Needs `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh`.

## A refused read is asked once more (Oct 22, 2026)

`liveRequest` (`functions/kalshiLiveLib.js`) asks a GET that Kalshi answers with 429 once more after 1.2 seconds. An order (any POST) is still never retried, which is what gate L10 protects and test RR1 proves. Why: the balance and position reads share one key with the exit watch's 10 second loop, and one refused read skipped a minute's entries and put "The account could not be read: HTTP 429" on the Bot page. Needs `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh` and `bash functions/deploy-push.sh`.

## The quote recorder pilot (Oct 22, 2026, research only)

`.github/workflows/record-quotes.yml` and `scripts/record_quotes.py` record Kalshi's 15 minute crypto quotes (Bitcoin, Ethereum, Solana, XRP, Dogecoin; best bid, ask and sizes about twice a second in the last 150 seconds of each window) next to the public trade feeds of Coinbase, Kraken and Bitstamp (the 10 minutes before each close), plus each market's result, a per-window coverage count and an hourly clock check. It exists for the research session's RECORD1 (do Kalshi's quotes lag spot in the last two minutes). It is a ONE DAY PILOT first: its job is to show how many windows a free runner catches (late scheduled jobs, Kalshi's public read limit) before anyone commits to 14 days. It holds no key and no secret, places no order, reads only public data, and the built-in token only pushes gzipped files to the `quote-data` branch, never to main (`test/quoteRecorderGates.test.js`). Nothing about the bot changes.

**The recorder was extended (Oct 28, 2026, the owner: "extend the recorder", for the research session's no-view brief on Bitcoin, Ethereum and gold).** `scripts/record_quotes.py` now also records gold's 15 minute market (`KXGOLD15M`, in `quotes.csv` with the others) and, in the last 150 seconds before each hourly close, the near-the-money strikes (mid between 3c and 97c, at most 40 per series, polled about every 3 seconds) of the hourly Bitcoin and Ethereum ladders (`KXBTCD`, `KXETHD`) and gold's daily and weekly ladders (`KXGOLDD`, `KXGOLDW`, which close at 21:00 UTC) into `ladder.csv` with a `close_time` column. Same schedule, same `quote-data` branch, public data only, no key, no order; a refused read backs off. Tests Q7, Q8. It goes live on merge, and data starts arriving on the next recorder run.

**The order book recorder (Oct 29, 2026, the owner approved the research session's QUOTE2B spec; research only).** `.github/workflows/record-book.yml` and `scripts/record_book.py` record the Bitcoin hourly "above K" ladder (`KXBTCD`): for the 7 strikes nearest the money of the event closing at the next top of the hour, the best 5 YES bids and 5 NO bids with sizes every 3 seconds from 55 minutes before each close, plus the public trade tape every 30 seconds (trade ids de-duplicated), with the server's Date header on every row so clock skew can be measured. Public endpoints only, no key, no order; the built-in token pushes gzipped files to the `quote-data` branch (under `book/`), never to main. Same 4 hour back to back job pattern as the quote recorder. It exists so a forward maker test (MAKER5 or QUOTE2B) can use a real queue model; about 100 MB gzipped over 14 days. Not a 15 minute series, so it cannot hold a position with L1. Tests B1 to B6 in `test/bookRecorderGates.test.js`. Goes live on merge; stop it by deleting the workflow.

**Recorder R6, the 15 minute book and tape (Oct 29, 2026, the owner approved the research session's spec `RECORDER_SPEC_R6_15MIN_BOOK_2026-10-29.md`; research only, nothing trades).** `.github/workflows/record-15m-book.yml` and `scripts/record_15m_book.py` record the whole 15 minute window of the five markets the bot trades (`KXGOLD15M`, `KXBTC15M`, `KXETH15M`, `KXDOGE15M`, `KXXRP15M`, fixed in code; XRP is paused in the bot but still recorded): every 2 seconds one batched public book read (`/markets/orderbooks?tickers=...`, falling back to one read per market after 3 failed batches in a row) keeping the best 5 YES and 5 NO bids with sizes, the server's Date header and the send and receive time; about 60 seconds after each close every public trade of that window, following the cursor until it is empty and de-duplicated by trade id (the id is not stored); a coverage row per window per market, written even for a window with no good poll (polls, 429s, fallback, largest gap, tape pages, trades, duplicates dropped, summed size next to Kalshi's volume and the result); an hourly SNTP clock check. Public endpoints only, no key, no auth header, no order; two request streams, at least 0.5 seconds between book calls and 1.5 between tape pages, a 429 waits 2 seconds and doubles up to 30. The built-in token pushes gzipped CSV to the `quote-data` branch under `book15r6/<date>/<HHMM>/`, never to main. It is triggered every 30 minutes under one concurrency group that never cancels a running job (scheduled starts run hours late), and each job records up to the next 4 hour boundary, so a dropped trigger costs at most 30 minutes; the window already open when a job starts is joined late (its tape is still complete). **It stops by itself after 14 days**: from 2026-11-13 23:59 UTC every run exits at once (checked in the workflow and in the script). Stop it sooner by deleting the workflow. Estimated (the spec's numbers): about 45 MB a day gzipped, about 630 MB over the 14 days, about 35 public reads a minute. **It replaces R2** (`record-book15.yml` and `record_book15.py`, Bitcoin, Ethereum and gold every 3 seconds under `book15/`, #610), whose files were removed in the same change, so the same markets are never read twice; what R2 recorded before the merge stays on `quote-data` under `book15/`. Tests FB1 to FB12 in `test/fifteenBookRecorderGates.test.js`.

Answers go gzipped to its own `kalshi-pull-data` branch, never main, never the site. The owner confirmed on Nov 12, 2026 that the waiver of Kalshi's Developer Agreement and Terms of Use COVERS BOTH Kalshi hosts (`api.elections.kalshi.com` and `external-api.kalshi.com`). The first jobs ship and DO NOT RUN: the approval NO-side pull, the sports spread cell (waiting for its event list), the ROLLOFF proxy check and the COMBO1 open book; ECONLAG and BALLOTNO are not included. ## kalshi-pull perps route (Nov 12, 2026, the owner: pull Kalshi's Bitcoin and Ethereum perpetual minute candles)

The research desk wants `KXBTCPERP` and `KXETHPERP` 1 minute candles from 2026-06-03 to Nov 12. Chosen route: extend the ONE reviewed runner (`scripts/kalshi_pull.py`), not a second workflow. **The runner now has a two-row table, `ROUTES`**: `elections` (`api.elections.kalshi.com`, `/trade-api/v2`) and `perps` (`external-api.kalshi.com`, `/trade-api/v2/margin`, copied exactly from `scripts/record_perps_candles.py`). A job names a row by KEY (`"route": "perps"`) and may not carry `host` or `base_path` when it does; the five older jobs have no `route` key and still pin the elections pair by value. A `perps` job may read ONE thing: `/markets/{KXBTCPERP|KXETHPERP}/candlesticks` with exactly `period_interval=1`, `start_ts`, `end_ts`, one page, a window of at most 86,400 seconds; no list, no funding, no cursor, no series. Everything else is unchanged and applies to both routes: GET only, no key, no redirect, at most 1 request a second, three 429s end the run, the deny list (which still refuses any path containing `/margin`, `/orders`, `/portfolio`), caps, size limits, 6 runs a day. Why not a separate workflow: it would copy the pacing, 429 and push code into a second place that has to be reviewed again; a table row adds one host and one path pattern to the one that is already gated. **Counts:** 163 UTC days (June 3 to Nov 12 inclusive), one request per day per ticker, so 163 requests for each of `PERPBTC1M` and `PERPETH1M`, 326 in all, about 3 runner minutes each at one a second. The per-call candle limit is NOT documented in this repo; the request size is the one the existing recorder already uses (86,400 seconds, 1,440 one minute candles), so no larger window is assumed. **`PERPETH1M` is paused (Head of R&D, Nov 12): no registered test uses it, it must not be dispatched, and the runner refuses it (status `paused`, not `active`).** Size watch: 1,440 candles a day x 163 days may reach the 200 MB per-run cap (a clean `stopped_size` that keeps what was fetched), so check the first real run's manifest before any second ticker. If the first real run shows a day with a short answer, stop and read the manifest before any wider window. **Runner changes are security-relevant**: review `ROUTES`, `route_of`, `check_perps_job` and `check_perps_request`. The Kalshi terms read was waived by the owner (owner-accepted risk, see the kalshi-pull section above); this is NOT a finding that automated reads are allowed. The owner confirmed on Nov 12, 2026 that the waiver of Kalshi's Developer Agreement and Terms of Use COVERS BOTH Kalshi hosts (`api.elections.kalshi.com` and `external-api.kalshi.com`). Gates: `test/kalshiPullGates.test.js` KP22 to KP25. Docs: `docs/KALSHI_PULL.md`. No job has run.

## Combo book recorder (Nov 12, 2026, the owner approved; research only)

`.github/workflows/record-combo.yml` and `scripts/record_combo.py` record Kalshi combo (multivariate, exchange_index 1) markets so a paper test of buying either side of a combo can be priced with real depth: every 30 minutes the quotes of the open combos with any posted bid or ask at any price on either side, or any volume (a sample of at most 5,000 listed; widened Nov 12, 2026 from a yes price of 0.30 or more, so the cheap side and the house side are both testable), the best 5 YES and 5 NO bids with sizes of the 40 with the tightest yes spread, the public trades of the 15 most traded, and once a day the settled combos that traded. Public GET only, no key, no order, no Firestore; plain User-Agent; a 429 backs off and three in a row stop the run; at most 90 requests a run, 6 seconds apart (at most 10 a minute, at most 4,320 a day). Gzipped data goes to its own `combo-data` branch (never main), is not published with the site, and it stops by itself on 2027-01-31. About 0.7 MB a day gzipped (about 15 KB a run measured Nov 12 with the widened filter) and 310 to 430 runner minutes a day (full table in `docs/COMBO_RECORDER.md`). Goes live on merge. Tests CB1 to CB10 in `test/comboBookRecorderGates.test.js`.

**Expectation and stop rule.** An earlier pass found no resting ask on any sampled combo, so it may capture little. Read on or after 14 days of recording: if fewer than 20 distinct combos ever showed a two-sided quote with a bid on the side a paper test would buy, or fewer than 100 combo trades printed, delete `record-combo.yml` and report that combos cannot be priced (`docs/COMBO_RECORDER.md`).

## Hourly balance snapshot (Nov 12, 2026, the owner)

**Why.** The bank-side check of which booking basis is right: over a window with no deposit or withdrawal, the change in the account's cash should equal the orders' booked result. That needs the balance recorded on a clock, not only when the Bot page is open. **What is stored.** One document an hour in `kalshiBalanceSnaps` (id is the UTC hour, for example `2026-11-12T14`): `ts` (the hour start), `at` (the read time), `total` (dollars) and `shards` (`[{shard, dollars}]`, from Kalshi's `balance_breakdown`; if Kalshi ever sends only the top-level balance, `shards` is empty and `total` is that figure read as cents, the same shaping the Bot page's account read uses, `shapeBalance`). No order id, client order id, position, fill or key. **Design.** A new small scheduled function, `kalshiBalanceSnapshot` (`functions/kalshiBalanceSnapshotLib.js`, every 60 minutes), chosen over adding a write to `runL1Tick` so the tick, the exit watch and their off-equivalence fixtures (SW0, SI0) are untouched and no order path can be affected. It makes ONE GET of `/portfolio/balance` through `liveRequest` with the same two secrets the account function already holds, no POST, no retry beyond `liveRequest`'s existing single re-ask of a 429. Two instances in one hour write the same document id with a merge. Every error is swallowed; a failed read leaves a gap in the series, which is acceptable. **Retention.** 90 days: in the 03:00 UTC run only, it deletes at most 200 documents older than that. **Rules.** `kalshiBalanceSnaps`: admin read, no client write (like `kalshiLiveControl`). **Report.** The nightly report has a `balanceHistory` section (`count`, `rows` of `ts`, `total`, `shards`, oldest first, the asked window, at most 2160 rows; an empty history is an empty list; a failed read omits the field). The report still holds no Kalshi key and only reads. It changes no order, size, stop or risk behaviour. **What it cannot do:** deposits, withdrawals, fees outside orders and open positions (cash leaves the balance while a position is open) all move the balance, so compare over a window that starts and ends with nothing open, and with no deposit. Needs `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh` (it now lists `functions:kalshiBalanceSnapshot`), `bash functions/deploy-report.sh` (the report section), and `npx firebase-tools deploy --only firestore:rules --project shwoopnet` (the rules). `deploy-latest.sh` runs the first two in order and names the rules line. Tests BS1 to BS8 (`test/kalshiBalanceSnapshotGates.test.js`).

## The second strategy slot (Oct 29, 2026, phase A, inert)

*Superseded, see "State as of Nov 13, 2026" near the top; kept as the record of what was decided then.*

The owner wants more than one live strategy, but only through a reviewed, fail-closed path, and nothing has passed the research bar yet. Phase A is the guard rails only: `functions/kalshiSlotLib.js`, `test/kalshiSlotGates.test.js`, `docs/SECOND_SLOT_DESIGN.md`. It is INERT: `SLOT_ENABLED` is `false`, `SLOT_STRATEGIES` is a frozen empty array, nothing in `functions/` or `index.html` imports the library, it has no network, Firestore, clock or signing code, and `kalshiLiveLib.js`, `index.js` and `firestore.rules` are untouched. **The owner's direction rule (Oct 29, 2026): a slot order may sit in the same market and window as an L1 order when it is on the SAME side. It would only be wrong if it were HEDGING.** So the opposite side is blocked in both directions (slot against L1 or slot, L1 against slot), fails closed on any unreadable order list, lock or position, and is decided by a lock `kalshiLiveDirection/{ticker}` read and written in the same transaction that creates the order document. A slot may send more than one contract and scale in, inside hard caps in code; the slot's dollar caps SCALE WITH THE SIZING BASE (owner, Oct 29, 2026: the gold ladder makes L1 bigger as the account grows): `SLOT_CAP_FRACTIONS` (per order 1%, per window 2%, open total 3%, daily 2%, lifetime 6%) capped by `SLOT_CAP_CEILINGS` ($20, $40, $60, $20, $60) through `slotCaps(sizingBase)`, which is all zero, so no order, for an unusable base. It shares ONE risk budget with L1: slot dollars count against the same 5.5% window cap and open slot orders count as lost in the same 12% stop (the later wiring must change `runL1Tick` and `botRisk`; this phase documents and tests the contract: `slotRiskForL1`, `slotRiskForBotRisk`, `slotWindowSpent`). Each engine sells only its own contracts, under the shared `claimClose` lease, never more than the account holds. Nothing stored can add or loosen a strategy; kill rules latch off and only a reviewed edit re-arms. Review round (Oct 29, 2026): one entry gate only (`slotOrderCheck`, no flags, needs a `lockTxn` proof), the daily and lifetime caps are checked against the new order's own cost, a stop-out is a loss in the shared stop the moment it is sold, scale-in stops after any exit, a close sells nothing for settled, hand-closed or errored orders and never L1's contracts (`othersUnsold`), kill rules are sticky and re-armed only by a later `reviewedAt`, and the window figure for L1 is the gross `windowSpent`. The wiring checklist is in the design doc. Admitting a strategy is a later, separate, reviewed pull request (phases B to H in the design doc).

**Phase B (Oct 29, 2026): wired, still off.** The slot is wired into the running bot behind constants only: entries need `slotActive` (`SLOT_ENABLED === true && SLOT_STRATEGIES.length > 0`), and everything that protects an open slot position (exits, settle sweep, the shared stop and window cap, the direction lock, L1's clamp) runs on the wider `slotWatch` (`slotActive() || SLOT_EVER_USED`), so switching the slot off or deleting a strategy never orphans a live position (`SLOT_ON` in `functions/index.js`, `slotOn(args)` in `kalshiLiveLib.js`; nothing stored, requested or typed on the page can feed either; there is no test backdoor). The exit pass also sells a slot order that ends up against another order's contracts (a hedge from an old revision during a deploy). Review round and prerequisites for the first admission: `docs/SECOND_SLOT_WIRING.md`, "Review round 2". While it is false the deployed functions make exactly the reads, writes and orders they made before: `test/kalshiSlotWiringGates.test.js` SW0 (the library) and `test/kalshiSlotIndexWiringGates.test.js` SI0 (`index.js`) replay recorded call logs, and **a change to either fixture means the off-equivalence broke: fix the code, not the fixture**. The one legitimate exception: `offEquivalenceIndex.json` was re-recorded on Oct 29, 2026 from `origin/main`'s own `index.js` (a scratch checkout with no slot code, same harness) after #613 added the exit watch heartbeat write, and the merged branch's output was required to be byte-identical to that recording; `offEquivalence.json` needed no re-record because #613 and #615 change nothing in its scenarios. A re-record is valid only from main's code and only when the branch matches it byte for byte. New files: `functions/kalshiSlotRunLib.js` (tick, exits, settle sweep, all I/O injected) and `functions/kalshiSlotStoreLib.js` (Firestore side, database injected). Slot orders live in `kalshiSlotOrders`, the direction lock in `kalshiLiveDirection/{ticker}` (written only inside the transaction that creates an order, for both engines), the kill latch in `kalshiSlotControl/killed`; the first two are admin read and no client write in `firestore.rules`, the latch has no rule. No new secret, scheduled function, callable or endpoint. The page and the alerts read only `kalshiLiveOrders`, so a slot order is never shown or alerted as an L1 order (and its fills show as unmatched account activity until the page card exists, which must come before the first admission). Deploy: `functions/deploy-sizing.sh` (minute function), `functions/deploy-push.sh` (exit watch and Flatten), `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules`; see `docs/SECOND_SLOT_WIRING.md` for the full note.

## The strategy registry (Nov 12, 2026, the owner: the Strategies page should be "a constant rotating thing whenever we add or remove strategies")

`strategies-registry.json` at the site root is the ONE list of every strategy and its stage (Idea, Shadow, Paper, Probe, Live, Retired). Research updates it by a reviewed edit; the Strategies page fetches it once per page load (same origin, one GET) and draws it beside what it computes from the private data: pipeline rows (Idea, Shadow, Paper, Probe) as compact cards in the Shadow lane (OICORE and EVENT830 are registered Probe, matching the lifecycle doc, and keep their computed cards in the Live lane), retired rows not already in the Retired list appended to it. It never replaces a computed live card. **Details (the owner picked option A, Nov 12: "i like. A"):** each card is one line and a Details button that opens a small popup showing only what it does in one line, the kill rule, the return rule, room left (a count toward `roomTarget`, from the page's own private shadow read, never from the file) and one small chart; no data draws no chart and says so. Each idea, shadow or paper row is labelled UNPROVEN and carries its n (only from public data, and `nBasis` says what it counts), start date, next read date and its kill and return rules in plain words; anything not verified says "to confirm with the Head of R&D" in `notes`. **It holds no live figures** (it is public: Pages serves it; `test/strategyRegistryGates.test.js` RG4 runs the no-live-figures phrasings over it). **The rotation guarantee:** a rule added to `SHADOW_RULES` or a strategy added to `SLOT_STRATEGIES` without a registry row naming it in `code` fails RG3, so the page cannot show "Shadow 0" while rules log. The page merges, it does not own the live figures; the old hard-coded retired rows stay until moved. Page change only: no order, no bot change, nothing to deploy. Tests RG1 to RG5. **Lane list matched to the Head of R&D (Nov 12, 2026, the EA for the owner):** the sports spread cell is Retired after its FAIL, MMSIM1 stays Retired, and the Bitcoin settlement-index lead shows only as "CFRTI-K rebuild, read from Nov 20" (the book and spot variants are folded into it); the Shadow lane keeps GOLDOFF, approval NO side, combo house side, spot-lead and NC15DEPTH1.

## How to talk to the owner (standing rules, set 2026-10-20, updated 2026-10-21)

- Keep replies short and plain. Lead with the result and the numbers. Assume the owner remembers nothing from earlier in the conversation, so say where a thing is and what it is.
- **Offer choices with the pop-up menu (the AskUserQuestion tool), not a numbered list typed into the reply** (the owner, 2026-10-21: "from now on in this project"). Put the recommended option first and mark it "(Recommended)". A closing line of text is still fine for what happens next, but the choices themselves go in the pop-up.
- Never use em dashes. Use they/them for anyone whose pronouns are not stated.
- Never ask for, or accept, credentials, keys or sign-in codes in chat.
- Stats to the owner in the short form (orders filled, won/lost, profit factor) unless they ask for more. Times are always US Central.
- Merge a pull request only when the owner says so. Keep the current look: no new design system (see Conventions).

## One Overall Bot page (Oct 30, 2026, the owner: "build the entire page to be overall ... identifiers ... one module that displays the unique strategies"; replaces the strategy switch)

There is no strategy switch and no per-strategy panel. Every card shows every order the bot placed (L1's and the second slot's, `kalshiAllOrders`), a small chip (`kalshiChipHtml`, `kalshiStrategyOf`) on each open position and recent trade says which strategy it came from, and one **Strategies** module (`#kalStrategies`, `renderKalshiStrategies`) lists L1 and each registered second strategy: status, today, result, settled, win rate, open, last order. The Account result line and Stop Loss and Risk count both. `KAL_SLOT_REGISTERED` and `KAL_STRATEGY_INFO` are labels only; gate ST6 ties them to the bot's code (the registry, and the markets L1 trades). Page only. Tests ST1 to ST7.

## Strategies live in the Account card (Oct 30, 2026, the owner: "id do j, but move the all strategies text to the top")

The separate Strategies card is gone. The table (`#kalStrategies`, `renderKalshiStrategies`) is a child of the Account card (`data-card="account"`), on the right beside the balance, Today and the Loss stop, which sit on the left. **All strategies is the FIRST row** (the account figure; the strategies are its parts) and each strategy's markets are a small line under its name (`kal-strat-mk`; on a phone the table stays a stack of cards with a Markets row). It is no longer in the Layout menu (`OPTIONAL`), and the default flow is account, status, result line, positions, trades. Tests Y2, Y5, Y5b, ST14. The tiles and tallies rule (ST13) still holds: every card that counts orders reads `kalshiAllOrders()`.

## Ethereum paused, L1E removed, Dogecoin on gold's ladder (Oct 30, 2026, the owner: "we can just remove L1E altogether", then "lets allow dogecoin to follow the same gold ladder. i want these to be the same strategy entirely")

*Superseded, see "State as of Nov 13, 2026" near the top; kept as the record of what was decided then.*

`PAUSED_SERIES` is now XRP, Bitcoin, Ethereum and the probes. Ethereum stays in `L1_SERIES` (names, quotes, alerts, exit watch still sells an open position), has no resume date, and the L1E label and row are gone (every L1 order is `L1`). Why: ETHFUNNEL showed Ethereum rarely fires because the smooth-climb step removes 95.5% of in-band windows. `CAPPED_SERIES` is Bitcoin, Ethereum, XRP; `FIXED_CONTRACTS` is XRP 1 and natural gas 2. Dogecoin left both lists, so it takes the same sizing as gold: `l1Count` with the session's `sizeCap`, the 2% base, the add-on capped at a quarter of base, the 5.5% window cap, the 12% stop and the 25c exit, up to `L1_ORDER_CEILING` (the size lock now allows Dogecoin that ceiling). It is no longer part of the one-crypto-order-per-window rule, so it can trade in the same window as gold. Risk in plain numbers: one Dogecoin early-sell loss at the gold size is the same dollars as a gold one. Review rule to set with the owner (kill: go back to 5 fixed if Dogecoin's last 100 settled orders lose more than 8% or net under +0.5c a contract). Exchange index: all live markets (gold, Bitcoin, Ethereum, XRP, Dogecoin) are on shard 2 per the owner's export Oct 30, 2026; older "shard 0: gold" text above is stale. Tests GF1, GF3, OC1, FS1 to FS3, OS1, PX1, SZ1 to SZ6, OV2, ST6, ST8, ST15, ST16, SF2. Needs `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh`.

`L1_SERIES` is now gold, XRP, Bitcoin, Dogecoin, Ethereum: AUD/USD, USD/CAD and natural gas (the probe markets, paused since Oct 28 with no way back, no orders since) left it, which also removes three market reads from every bot minute. ## Dogecoin back to a fixed 5 (Nov 3, 2026, the owner: "fixed 5 for dogecoin i guess")

*Superseded, see "State as of Nov 13, 2026" near the top; kept as the record of what was decided then.*

`FIXED_CONTRACTS` has `KXDOGE15M: 5` again, and `runL1Tick` treats any series in that list as a fixed size (no ladder step, no add-on), so `maxContractsFor` allows Dogecoin at most 5. Dogecoin keeps gold's other rules (window cap, 12% stop, 25c exit, around the clock); only the size is fixed. Why: at ladder size (11 to 15 contracts, since Oct 30) it had a run of early sells and one day lost several times the previous worst day; at 3 to 5 contracts its loss rate was lower and its result positive. The refinement chat's scan (Nov 3) found no other common feature in the losses (side, hour, spread, depth all flat); price is the only one, and the 90c to 92c bucket is C1's forward test. The Dogecoin review warning on the Bot page counts only orders before Nov 3 14:00 UTC (`KAL_DOGE_LADDER_TO`), since its rule (go back to 5) is now carried out. Putting Dogecoin back on the ladder is a reviewed edit. Tests FS1, FS3, OS1, SZ5, SF2, A4. Needs `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh`.

## Gold size held at 15 (Nov 3, 2026, the owner: "keep, but freeze growth")

`L1_SIZE_CEILING` is 15 (was 40), so the balance-driven size of gold stops at 15 contracts whatever the balance. Why: the owner does not want L1 to grow ("high winrate, little to no reward": a win pays 1c to 4c a contract, an early sell loses 60c to 90c); the refinement chat found nothing in gold that is materially more profitable, and its live depth data covers orders up to 28 contracts only. The profit add-on (at most a quarter of the base, so up to 3 more), the 5.5% window cap, the 12% stop, the 25c exit and the order ceiling (50) are unchanged. At the current balance the ladder was at about 15, so today's orders change little; the freeze removes future steps. Raising it again is a reviewed edit. Page text (Size note, Start confirmation) says 15; test L1 gates read the constant. Needs `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh`.

## Gold L1 kill latch (Nov 10, 2026, live; the owner confirmed it)

The owner: "give gold L1 a coded KILL and RETURN". Full design, the options weighed and the gaps: `docs/GOLD_KILL_LATCH.md`. **KILL (latch, cash basis, fees included):** gold stops placing new orders, with no automatic restart, at a drawdown from its own cash peak of $58.75, a FIXED dollar line (`GOLD_KILL.drawdownDollars`, set from the sizing base when written; re-set only by a reviewed edit when the base grows; the replay never reads the sizing base) or a cash net under -$20 after 40 trades, both counted from `GOLD_KILL.rearmAt` (2026-11-10, `functions/kalshiGoldKillLib.js`). **ALERT only:** rolling-60 cash PF under 0.7, rolling-100 under 1.0, 3 early sells in 40 trades write `gold alert` events (warning in the notification center); a kill writes a `gold kill` event (critical). **How, without touching the fixtures:** a separate scheduled function `kalshiGoldKillCheck` (every minute, no key, no order) REPLAYS gold's settled orders and caches the verdict as `kalshiLiveControl/sizing.goldKill`; `runL1Tick` only looks at that field on the sizing state it already received (`goldKillActive`) and drops gold from `tradable`, so it makes no new read or write and `offEquivalence.json` and `offEquivalenceIndex.json` are byte identical. **It only removes gold orders:** the exit watch never looks at the latch and still sells an open gold position at 25c; sizes, bands, the 12% stop and the other strategies are unchanged. **RETURN:** only a reviewed edit that moves `GOLD_KILL.rearmAt` past the latch (and, after a kill, sets `rearmIsReturn`, which sends the first 30 trades at half size); the bar is shadow GOLDALL PF 1.3 or better over 60 or more trades and 7 days with the second half at 1.3 or better, then the owner's approval. That bar may keep a normal gold out (gold's long run live PF is only modestly above that bar); the cost is idle gold, not lost money. Nothing stored or typed on the page can disable or loosen it. **Read cost:** the checker reads only orders since `rearmAt` (about 10 to 15 gold orders a day, so about 10,000 reads a day early, about 40,000 at 30 days; the alerts use that same set, so they need 60 or 100 trades since the re-arm). Known gaps: the tick's full write of the sizing document can overwrite a fresh latch (the next minute's replay re-derives it identically, since the line is a constant), no phone push yet, the thresholds are untested on a real failure. Four lists of scheduled functions in the gates were extended on purpose for the new function. Tests K1 to K13 in `test/kalshiGoldKillGates.test.js`. Needs `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh` and `bash functions/deploy-push.sh`.

## OICORE limit 1c above the touch (Nov 3, 2026, the owner: "build 1")

`SLOT_LIMIT_SLIP = { OICORE: 0.01 }` and `slotLimitFor` in `functions/kalshiSlotLib.js`: OICORE's order limit is the side's touch plus 1c (never past its priceMax, at most 2c for any strategy, every other strategy unchanged). The band test stays on the quoted price; the gate and its caps cost the order at the higher limit; for a NO buy the YES price in the body moves 1c the other way. Why: the refinement chat's S-LIM1 read (a quarter of limits went unfilled when the ask moved up 1c); the sample is tiny, so it is a probe of the rule. Review: after 20 orders that filled above the price first seen, revert if their net is below zero. Tests LM1 to LM3, and the OICORE end to end gate now expects a 19c limit on an 18c touch. Needs `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh`.

## EVENT830, the release day probe (Nov 3, 2026, a second strategy in the second slot; below the research bar)

`SLOT_STRATEGIES` now holds OICORE and `EVENT830` (`functions/kalshiSlotLib.js`; pre-registration `kalshi-scalper/README.md#SLOT-EVENT830`; research record `workers/EVENT830_REGISTRATION.md` on the research branch). Only in the 15 minute window that CLOSES at a listed US 8:30 AM ET release (13:30 UTC, 7:30 AM Central): 3 to 2 minutes before the close, if the YES spread is 10c or less, it buys the cheaper side at 5c to 50c (hard limit 50c, no slip), 1 contract, held to settlement, in eight L1-free markets (KXSILVER15M, KXCOPPER15M, KXWTI15M, KXEURUSD15M, KXGBPUSD15M, KXUSDJPY15M, KXHYPE15M, KXBNB15M). The dates are `SLOT_CALENDARS.EVENT830`, frozen in code (15 windows, Nov 5 to Dec 23, 2026; both the close Kalshi reports and the ticker's Eastern window code must match a row); nothing stored can add one, and a later date is a reviewed edit from official schedules. The eight markets are reserved to this id (`SLOT_SERIES_RESERVED`), and `SLOT_HOLD_ALLOWED` is OICORE and EVENT830. New optional entry fields `maxSpread` and `calendar` (the strategy's own id). The minute function reads the eight markets only when `slotCalendarSeriesDue` says a listed window is about to enter (about 8 public market reads at 13:27 UTC on a release day, none on any other minute; no Firestore read), into a copy of the quotes that only the slot tick sees. Kill rules as close as the validator can express them: net below $0 at any point from 40 settled fills (PF under 1.0; the owner's 1.2 line cannot be written and must be read by hand), 20 losses in a row; the -$15 floor is not separate (40 fills at 50c can lose at most $20.80). The slot's own loss stop (`RISK_PROFILES.SECOND`) is one budget over both slot strategies, so the Bot page's Own stops line now shows L1 and one figure, `OICORE + EVENT830` (test OM1). Shard note: the three FX markets are on shard 0 and send nothing until it holds money. Live it enters at the quote about 177 s before the close, a minute earlier than the research fill (the slot cannot enter under 120 s). `SLOT_LAST_REVIEWED_ON` and `reviewedAt` are 2026-11-05; forward read 2026-12-07. Tests EV1 to EV9 (`test/event830SlotGates.test.js`). **Deploy in this order:** `bash functions/deploy-push.sh` FIRST (an older exit watch would give an unknown slot order the 25c fallback stop and sell it), then `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh`, both before 13:27 UTC on Nov 5 for the first window.

## FLOW1, a seller-flow probe (Nov 4, 2026, the owner: "can probably go live")

*Retired, see "State as of Nov 13, 2026" near the top (FLOW1 was removed Nov 4, 2026, see "FLOW1 removed"); kept as the record of what was decided then.*

`SLOT_STRATEGIES` adds `FLOW1`: Bitcoin 15 minute, the cheaper side 5c to 50c, 180 to 120 s before the close, 5 contracts, held to settlement, ONLY when the taker flow on that side over 360 to 180 s before the close is at or below -0.036 (new optional entry field `flowMax`; flow = (contracts takers bought on the side minus contracts they sold) / total, every public trade counted, `flowFromTrades` in `functions/kalshiSlotRunLib.js`, at most 5 pages of the public `/markets/trades` tape; fewer than 5 trades in the span, a failed read or a partial read sends nothing, as S-FLOW1 dropped windows under 5 trades). Lead: the refinement chat's S-FLOW1 on 146 windows (Oct 29 to Nov 3): low-flow half win 36.8%, PF 2.54, +13.25c a contract; high half PF 0.68; permutation p 0.0026; independent of open interest, price change, entry price and spread. Only 6 days of data and no named mechanism: BELOW the research bar; it went live before the forward read (S-FLOW1, about 60 to 120 windows) and the research session's history test (FLOW1) finish, at the owner's word. It shares the slot's per-market open-contract cap, so in a window OICORE also trades, OICORE (listed first) takes the room. Kill: 15 losses in a row, net under -$10 after 40 fills, the slot caps; forward read 2026-11-20. `SLOT_HOLD_ALLOWED` is OICORE, EVENT830, FLOW1. The order records `flow`. Page: `KAL_SLOT_REGISTERED`, `KAL_STRATEGY_INFO` ("Seller flow"), the `kalRulesSlotFlow` sentence (SF3 checks it names Bitcoin only), the changelog. Tests FL1 to FL5 (`test/flow1SlotGates.test.js`). Needs `bash functions/deploy-latest.sh`.

## FLOW1 removed (Nov 4, 2026, both research chats recommended it)

`FLOW1` is out of `SLOT_STRATEGIES` and `SLOT_HOLD_ALLOWED`; the `flowMax` entry field and `flowFromTrades` stay in code. Why: the research session's registered FLOW1 history test (2,568 Bitcoin windows, Aug 6 to Oct 2, scored once) found the low-flow cheap side at PF 1.13 to 1.23 and the HIGH-flow side better in the second half (low minus high -3.6c, within-day permutation p 0.49); a per-window diff showed no data bug, only that the refinement chat's six replication days were a favourable slice (the other 28 days: low PF 1.04, high 1.52); the refinement chat's second out-of-sample block (Aug 12 to Sep 9) agreed (low about -0.5c). Expected value about zero. Any FLOW1 order already placed is still exited and settled (`SLOT_EVER_USED`), and the page labels it "Seller flow (removed)". The shadow S-FLOW1 keeps scoring; a re-admission would be a reviewed edit after a forward read above PF 1.5 on 60+ low-half windows. Needs `bash functions/deploy-latest.sh`.

## Smoother Bot page layout (Nov 5, 2026, the owner: "just more of a blocky interaction.. it doesnt move smoothly, i don't have much say in where each card goes on my page"; they chose "Smooth + free placement", no saved layouts yet)

The Bot page cards (account, status, pnl, positions, trades) are ONE ordered list on one two column grid: the rail and main wrappers and the slot dealing (`flow()`, CSS order, dense packing) are gone, so the page shows exactly the order the layout holds and any card can go anywhere. Each card is Half or Full (`data-w`; defaults reproduce the old look: account, positions and trades Full, status and pnl Half side by side; the Account card stacks its pieces at Half); on a phone (900px and under) it is one column and the width toggle is hidden. **Edit layout** (the Layout menu; it replaced the Unlock tick box) shows a bar with Undo, Reset, Cancel and Done and per-card controls (a grip, Half or Full, up and down arrows); Cancel puts back the layout from before editing and nothing is saved until Done. Dragging uses pointer events (a mouse after a few pixels; a touch after a 280 ms hold, so a swipe still scrolls, or at once on the grip) and the order changes only when the pointer passes another card's middle; neighbours glide with a 200 ms FLIP slide, off under the device reduce-motion setting and the page's `data-reduce-motion`. Every card head has a fold chevron (saved per card); the account card can fold, keeping its head with the Running pill, but can never be hidden. Moves are announced in an aria-live line. Saved under the same `kalBotLayout` key (this browser and the account) with `v: 2`, `order`, `hidden`, `width`, `folded`; an older copy (no version) is migrated by dealing its order the way the old page did, so it keeps its look, and the dead `cols` field is dropped. Gates: `test/layoutSmoothGates.test.js` L1 to L7; Y2 to Y6, Y9, Y13 (`kalshiLayoutGates`) and G24 (`kalshiSignalGates`) were rewritten on purpose for the new structure. Page only, live on merge.

## Slot skip reasons logged (Nov 5, 2026, the owner: "Save skip reasons")

The refinement chat found 8 of 44 OICORE triggers since Oct 30 with no live order and no record (Bitcoin windows closing Nov 1 08:45, Nov 2 08:45, 09:45 and 11:00, Nov 3 11:30 and 17:00, Nov 5 00:45 CT), and on Nov 5 13:30Z EVENT830 sent nothing for EUR/USD, GBP/USD and USD/JPY (shard 0, likely too little money there). The reasons only went to console logs. Now `runSlotTick` (`functions/kalshiSlotRunLib.js`) keeps one skip record per strategy and ticker for every market that is inside its strategy's entry span (the `jobs` list, so after the timing and calendar checks) and ends the tick without an order, and hands them to `args.recordSkip` AFTER the minute's orders are sent (or at a return that sends nothing), in parallel, every error swallowed. `index.js` passes `slotStoreLib.slotSkipWriter(db)`, which writes `kalshiSlotSkips/{strategy}_{ticker}` with a plain set (a full overwrite, not a merge, so the document holds the LATEST reason of that window and its own detail, never a mix of two minutes' fields; OICORE's span is one minute, so a window normally writes once). Fields: `strategy`, `ticker`, `reason` (a fixed code), `detail` (numbers only or null), `note` (the engine's sentence, at most 120 characters, or null), `atMs`, `secondsLeft`. Codes: `no_shared_risk`, `session_ended`, `halted`, `unresolved_order`, `orders_unreadable`, `preconditions_unreadable` (whole-tick returns, one record per in-span market), `market_unreadable` (detail http), `not_open`, `no_quote`, `not_listed`, `spread_wide` (spread, max), `price_out_of_band` (price, min, max), `no_oi_candle` (http), `oi_below` (oiRise, min), `flow_unreadable`, `flow_above`, `position_unreadable` (http), `scale_in`, `stale_tick` (lateSec), `record_failed`, `already_attempted`, and from the transaction's decision `shard_short` (shard, avail, need), `loss_stop`, `own_loss_stop` (need), `balance_unreadable`, plus the gate's refusals mapped by `checkReasonCode` to `killed`, `cap`, `direction_lock`, `depth_unknown`, `outside_window`, `oi_below`, `spread_wide`, `not_listed`, `scale_in`, `orders_unreadable`, else `gate` with the sentence in `note`. Nothing is written outside the span, for a market that got its order, or while the slot is off. Nothing that decides an order reads the collection: the engine never names it, the store only writes it, the only read is the nightly report. `firestore.rules`: admin read, no client write. The nightly report adds `skips` (`skipSection` in `functions/kalshiReportLib.js`): the last 7 days whatever `hours` says, `count`, `byReason` and up to 500 rows (`at`, `atMs`, `strategy`, `ticker`, `reason`, `secondsLeft`, numeric `detail`), never a document id; a failed read leaves the section out. The off-equivalence fixtures are untouched (the slot is off in every recorded scenario, so no skip write happens there). Tests K1 to K7 in `test/slotSkipReasonGates.test.js`. **Deploy:** `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh` (the minute function) and `bash functions/deploy-report.sh` (the report section), or `bash functions/deploy-latest.sh`, which selects both. The rule is deployed by none of those scripts (only `deploy-paper.sh` carries rules, with the paper tracker); run `npx firebase-tools deploy --only firestore:rules --project shwoopnet`. It only matters for reading the collection from the page; the server writes without it, and with no rule a browser is refused anyway. `deploy-latest.sh` now prints that command when `firestore.rules` changed. A `kalshiKeys` log of the order answer's key names was considered the same day and dropped: Kalshi's create-order answer is flat (order_id, fill_count, remaining_count, average_fill_price, average_fee_paid, ts_ms) and has no status, so `kalshiStatus` stays null and is left as it was.

## Resizable Bot cards (Nov 5, 2026, the owner: "this should maybe all be adjustable? size wise i guess")

In Edit layout every card has a bottom right corner (`.kal-resize`). Dragging it (pointer events; mouse and touch, no hold needed on the corner) changes the card's width and height; a dashed outline shows the snapped size while held and the size is applied on release as one Undo step. The Bot grid is now **12 columns** (`.kal-console`); `data-w` is a span, not a word: 3, 4, 6, 8 or 12 (a quarter, a third, a half, two thirds, full), and the defaults reproduce the old look (account, positions, trades 12; status, pnl 6). A height snaps to 40px steps, never below the card's head plus 120px of body (or its own height if smaller), and a card with a set height keeps its head and scrolls its body (`data-h`, `--kal-h`, a `.kal-card-body` wrapper made at load that holds the card's pieces; ids and listeners unchanged). The height only changes once the pointer has moved half a step vertically, so widening a card does not fix its height. Double click on the corner returns the card to Auto height (the default). The Half or Full button became a width button (1/4, 1/3, 1/2, 2/3, Full) and a height button (Auto, 240, 360, 480, 600) was added; both announce in the aria-live line. **The Account card is width only** (`NO_HEIGHT`): its Start, Pause and Flatten menu hangs below its head and a set height would clip it. A folded card ignores its height. At 900px and under every size, the buttons and the corner are ignored or hidden (one column, own height; the phone rule outranks the span rules). Saved in the same `kalBotLayout` object, now `v: 3`, `width` holding a span number and a new `height` map in px; a v2 copy migrates (`half` is 6, `full` is 12, which are the same widths) and keeps its look; Cancel and Undo cover resizes. **Not checked:** a real touch drag on a phone-size screen (the corner is hidden there, and on a tablet over 900px it uses the same pointer code as the mouse); a stylus. Gates: `test/layoutSmoothGates.test.js` L9 to L14, and L4, L5 and the old gates in `kalshiLayoutGates`, `kalshiSignalGates` were changed on purpose for spans. Page only, live on merge.

## The deploy wrapper missed the market list (Nov 5, 2026, the owner: "says nothing to deploy")

After #721 (EVENT830 position charts) `deploy-latest.sh` said "nothing to deploy", because its file rules never named `functions/kalshiLib.js` (the relay's series list, loaded by `kalshiBooks`), and it then moved its marker past the change. Now `kalshiLib`, `kalshiBookLib` and `kalshiSignalLib` plan `deploy-sizing.sh` (which deploys `kalshiBooks`), and any other changed `functions/*.js` that no rule claims is printed as a WARNING, so a missing rule can never read as nothing to deploy. #721 itself needs `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh` run by hand once. Test DL4.

## A folded card shows only its title (Nov 5, 2026, the owner: "only running should be visible when condensing these")

A folded Bot page card hides every control in its head (the Account result range buttons, the Recent trades filter) because they act on a body that is not shown; the Account card keeps its Running pill (`.kal-botmenu`: Start, Pause, Flatten). Folded cards take one padding on the head only, so all five are the same height (54px at desktop). Test L8 in `test/layoutSmoothGates.test.js`. Page only, live on merge.

## Perps 30 second recorder (Nov 5, 2026, the owner: "Faster data, then 10 to 20 tests", after Kalshi listed single stock perps)

Kalshi lists 88 perpetual markets (21 crypto, 67 others: single stocks, US Tech 100, SPY, QQQ, metals, oil). The hourly recorder gives about 300 snapshots a market in two weeks, too coarse for open, close or overnight tests, and the owner declined a mass generator on that data. `.github/workflows/record-perps-fast.yml` and `scripts/record_perps_fast.py` sample the same public list (same columns as `record_perps.py`) every 30 seconds from 09:10 to 10:30 and 15:40 to 17:00 New York time on weekdays (follows daylight saving), into gzipped CSV on the `perp-data` branch under `perps30/<date>/<open|close>.csv.gz`. Started every 20 minutes on weekdays; outside a window it exits at once, a second start for the same window does nothing, a failed read costs one sample. Research only: keyless GETs, the built-in token pushes only the data branch, no order. It stops by itself after 2027-02-26 (extended from 2026-12-07 on Nov 6, 2026; extend again by a reviewed edit). About 320 samples of 88 markets a weekday. V2 runs 10 to 20 registered tests on it after the Nov 6 resume. Tests PF1 to PF4 in `test/perpFastRecorderGates.test.js`. Goes live on merge.

## Perps cash price saver, and the 30 second recorder extended (Nov 6, 2026, V2's request, the owner: build both)

*Partly retired Nov 13, 2026 note: the cash price saver (`record-perps-cash.yml`, `scripts/record_perps_cash.py`) was deleted on Nov 11, 2026 because it read only Yahoo (see "Yahoo reads stopped on the research side"); the 30 second recorder part still runs. Kept as the record of what was decided then.*

V2's perps tests (open, close, overnight gap) compare a perp with the thing it tracks, and Yahoo keeps only about 7 days of 1 minute bars. Stock and ETF perps only started trading on Nov 5 at 18:39 UTC (12:39 PM CT; before that their bid was 0), so the stock side had to be copied before it expired. `.github/workflows/record-perps-cash.yml` and `scripts/record_perps_cash.py` save Yahoo's public 1 minute bars (pre and post market included) for every non-crypto symbol on Kalshi's public perps list (equities and ETFs by title, BRK.B as BRK-B; US500 as ^GSPC, US Tech 100 as ^NDX, US 2000 as ^RUT; gold, silver, copper, platinum, palladium, aluminum and WTI as their futures) plus ES=F, NQ=F and RTY=F, into `cash1m/<UTC date>.csv.gz` on the `perp-data` branch. The first run asks for the 7 day backfill (live test: 71 of 71 symbols, 304,039 bars, 4.4 MB, back to Oct 29), later runs for 2 days (overlap on purpose, dedupe on timestamp downstream). Started three times a night, Tuesday to Saturday UTC (02:00, 03:00, 04:00, after after-hours ends at 01:00 UTC); a second start the same day does nothing, a failed symbol costs only itself, and fewer than half the symbols read writes nothing so the next start retries. Research only: public GETs, no key, the built-in token pushes only `perp-data`, never main, six try push loop. It stops by itself after 2027-02-26. The 30 second perps recorder's stop date moved from 2026-12-07 to 2027-02-26 in the same change (V2 needs about 150 trades per test, which is about February). Tests PC1 to PC5 in `test/perpCashRecorderGates.test.js`; PF4 and `recorderPushRetryGates` updated. Goes live on merge; run it once by hand (Actions, record-perps-cash, Run workflow) right after the merge to take the backfill before more of the 7 days expires.

## OICORE size ladder (Nov 3, 2026, the owner: "yes build it"; held until the Nov 13 read)

`SLOT_LADDERS.OICORE` and `slotLadderState` in `functions/kalshiSlotLib.js`: OICORE may send 10, 12, 15, 20 or 30 contracts, the step REPLAYED from its own settled orders since `reviewedAt` every time (no stored step, so two instances agree and nothing on the page or in Firestore can raise it). Up one step after 20 fills and 7 days at the step (the owner chose faster steps the same day; it was 40 and 14) with win rate 20%+, net at least 1 x contracts dollars, profit factor 1.3+ and drawdown since the step began under 3 x contracts; down at once on a drawdown of 3 x contracts since the step began, or (40 fills at the step) win rate under 15% or net under -2 x contracts over the last 40. Each step needs a sizing base of $825 / $900 / $1,030 / $1,350 (refinement B4: a 95th percentile losing run plus an L1 bad day inside the 12% stop); a smaller base holds the size down, so at a small base OICORE stays at 10 even when the step is earned. For a laddered strategy the slot's daily loss cap is 2.5 x contracts dollars (at least the base cap, at most 6% of the base and $75; $25 at 10 contracts, was $16) and the lifetime loss cap is replaced by a drawdown stop of 5 x contracts dollars from its best result (at most 12% of the base and $150; $50 at 10), which refuses new orders until a reviewed edit moves `reviewedAt`. Per order, window and open caps, the depth share, the shared 12% stop, the 5.5% window cap and the kill rules are unchanged. The registry `contractsPerOrder` (10) and `SLOT_MAX_CONTRACTS_PER_ORDER` (10) stay the limit for every strategy without a ladder; `SLOT_LADDER_MAX_CONTRACTS` (30) is the ladder's ceiling. Why: the refinement chat's spec (depth is not the limit, at least 766 contracts within 1c in every sampled window; money at risk is). Untested live above 10 contracts. Tests LD1 to LD10 (`test/oicoreLadderGates.test.js`). Pre-registration: `kalshi-scalper/README.md#SLOT-OICORE`. Needs `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh` after merge.

## The report carries the real fill price and fee (Nov 6, 2026, the refinement chat's finding)

`avgFill` was null on every row of the nightly report: `kalshiReportLib.js` read `o.avgFill`, but the order documents store `averageFillPrice` and `averageFeePaid` (the POST answer's `average_fill_price` and `average_fee_paid`); only the page's "Copy all orders" export read the right fields. Rows (L1 and slot) now carry `avgFill` and a new `avgFee` from those fields (the old names still read, a missing value stays null). Why it matters: `settledPnl` books an order at its worst-case cost (`maxCost`, the limit price plus fee), by design, so a win is booked slightly low and a loss slightly high; for OICORE's early fills the limit sat about 1c a contract above the touch, so its reported net was understated if fills happened at the touch. With avgFill and avgFee in the report the exact result can be recomputed outside the bot; no figure the bot uses (stops, pool, sizing, the Account result) changed. Test R10; R2 and OV3 list the new key on purpose. Needs `bash functions/deploy-report.sh` (or `deploy-latest.sh`).

## Freshness fixes: OICORE's ladder (Nov 6, 2026)

The daily freshness check found the Start confirmation still saying "OICORE (10 contracts)" after #699 made OICORE a ladder (10 to start, then 12, 15, 20, 30). It now says "10 contracts to start, up to 30 as its own record earns it", and gate SF reads the start and top step from `SLOT_LADDERS.OICORE.steps` instead of pinning a fixed size. `docs/STRATEGIES.md`, `docs/PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md`, `docs/ROADMAP.md` and `docs/STRATEGY_RISK_SEPARATION.md` (step 3 marked built) say the same. Page and docs only, live on merge; no trading code changed.

## The fact checker agent (Nov 6, 2026, the owner: "do we have an agent fact checking both research and refinement chats")

`.claude/agents/fact-checker.md` (read only: Read, Grep, Glob, Bash; no Edit or Write): give it a claim from the research chat or the refinement chat with its numbers and sources. It splits the claim into checkable statements, checks "what is live" against the code on `origin/main` (never a note, and a merge date is not a deploy date), re-derives each figure from the code, a saved nightly report file, the recorder data and public reads, and answers CONFIRMED, WRONG or CANNOT_CHECK with its own numbers. It looks for the usual defects (look-ahead universes, a read held-out window, overlapping windows, untallied N, limit-booked against fill-booked figures). It never edits, deploys, merges, messages the other chats, prints a secret or judges whether to trade; the main session relays its answer and runs it on any finding that proposes a change or states what is live. Tests FC1 to FC4 in `test/factCheckerGates.test.js`. Agent file only, live on merge.

With `?orders=1` every L1 and slot row also has `exitAt` (ISO time of the early sell, null when none), `exitAvg` (the price it sold at, 0 to 1) and `exitBid` (the bid that triggered it). `runExitWatch` already stored them on the order; the report dropped them, so gold early sells could only be read through a candle proxy. Numbers and a time only, no exit order id; read only. Test R11 in `test/kalshiReportGates.test.js` (R2 and OV3 list the new keys on purpose). Needs `bash functions/deploy-report.sh` (or `deploy-latest.sh`).
