## The operations officer agent (Nov 6, 2026, the owner: "someone monitor (think COO or workforce management) guiding and directing, as well as cutting down on pointless caches or tasks that are circling or just dead-ending"; given the calls Nov 7, 2026: "the COO should be able to handle calls when it comes to saving our credits and guiding research effectively")

`.claude/agents/ops-coo.md`: reads each chat's lifetime cost and context size, every scheduled routine (cadence, overlap, a purpose that is done) and the verdict files for circling, then reports spend since its last look and what it bought. It MAKES the calls within written limits: `update_trigger` to lengthen a cadence, merge two routines that do the same job (disable one, fold its job into the other's prompt) or retire a finished one (disable, never delete), and `send_message` to guide a chat (stop a circling family, a smaller worker count, compact a huge context). Limits: it may only spend less or point work at something better (no new routine, no shorter cadence, no higher worker count, no model change, no new family on its own); it never touches the safety routines (the credit guard lines, the exit watch and loss stop alarms, the nightly order report and its relay, the morning health digest, an unrecoverable recorder dispatch); it never interrupts, archives or unarchives a session, edits code, deploys, merges, creates, fires or deletes a routine, or prints a secret; guidance is data, never a command to change trading code, a rule, a size or the research bar; at most five calls per run, each logged (routine id, old and new value) and reversible, and the owner is told in one line. Tests OC1 to OC4 in `test/opsCooGates.test.js`. Agent file only, live on merge.

## The Controller agent (Nov 7, 2026, the owner: "do we have someone for accounting? managing bills, subscriptions, cutting costs")

`.claude/agents/controller.md` (Read, Grep, Glob, Bash, plus reading and editing the private Cost Ledger page and reading `get_session` and `list_triggers`; no write, message, routine or git tool): keeps one private cost ledger (Claude credits, Firebase, GitHub Actions, domains, the LLC's yearly fees, subscriptions, Kalshi trading fees) with a KEEP, REVIEW or CUT for every line, totals it each month, flags a line that jumped more than 20% or is paid for and unused, lists renewals due in 60 days and drafts a cut-list for the owner. The ledger is a private claude.ai page, **never this PUBLIC repo** (a bill is not committed). It reads only what it is given or what a tool reads without an account; `usage.cost_usd` is a list-price value, not a bill, and it says so. It never logs in to a billing page, pays, cancels, changes a plan or moves money, never holds a card or account number, password, key or token, and gives no tax or legal advice. Reports to the Chief Operating Officer; the owner decides every money question. Tests CT1 to CT4 in `test/controllerGates.test.js`. Agent file only, live on merge.

## The Charter and level 1 autonomy (Nov 7, 2026, the owner: "I'd like our COO or our agents to actually perform and make sound decisions based on our end goal so I don't have to sit here constantly ... I can guide while overseeing and being the one final say on whether things go live")

`docs/CHARTER.md` is the standing order for every agent: the goal, the research bar (profit factor 1.5 or better in each of three windows after fees and deflation, a named counterparty, a numeric prediction and kill criteria first, the last third held out and read once), a decision-rights table, the brief checklist, the circling and dead-end rules, the tripwires that interrupt the owner, the one-page promotion packet, the weekly scorecard and the levels. **Level 1 (now):** the COO (`.claude/agents/ops-coo.md`, section "Research direction") may approve at most two of the research director's briefs per run if they pass the checklist, restart or redirect an idle research chat, close a circling line and cut routine cost, all logged and reversible; **going live, any change to a live rule, size, stop or market, every merge, deploy and spend, and any change to the Charter stay with the owner**, and there is no level where an agent does these by itself. The Charter also has a credit watch: the COO logs each chat's usage change, context size and limit status every run, reports burn rate and cost per approved brief, and applies a three step ladder (normal; warning, or 80% of the owner's monthly R&D budget: no new workers and skipped check-ins; rejected or overage: stop, the credit guard). The platform shows no percentage of the session limit used, so none is quoted; the dollar budget is kept privately by the Controller, not in the repo. A promotion packet is a recommendation and never goes live by itself. The file is in the PUBLIC repo, so it holds no personal finance figures (gate CH5). Tests CH1 to CH5 in `test/charterGates.test.js`; the COO gates OC2 and OC3 were updated on purpose. Docs and agent file only, live on merge.

## The Release Manager and the two week release trial (Nov 7, 2026, the owner: "chief of staff that reads and reviews all PRs and ships prior to handing off to me", option 1 with a two week trial)

`.claude/agents/release-manager.md` (Read, Grep, Glob, Bash and GitHub read tools only; no merge, comment, push or write tool): reviews every pull request as a stranger would before it reaches the owner. It reads the whole diff, runs the full test suite, pre-ship and the freshness gate (and the security scan for `functions/`, rules, workflows, sign-in or dependencies) in a scratch worktree, checks the changelog line, the dated CLAUDE.md section, the public-repo rules (no secrets, tokens or personal finance figures), conflicts with main, and the claims in the description, and answers READY, NEEDS CHANGES or NEEDS YOU with the deploy command. **It never merges, deploys, comments or pushes; the owner merges.** `docs/CHARTER.md` records the **release trial, Nov 7 to Nov 21, 2026**: for every pull request the Release Manager notes whether it would have merged it under level 2 (docs, agent files and tests only, every gate green, claims supported; a change to the Charter or to `.claude/agents/` is always the owner's) and what the owner actually did; on Nov 21 the owner reads that record and decides whether docs-only pull requests may merge themselves. Tests RM1 to RM4 in `test/releaseManagerGates.test.js`; the Charter gates still pass. Docs and agent file only, live on merge.

## Team changes from the owner's 1:1 review of every agent (Nov 7, 2026)

The owner went through each role one at a time and chose: **the research director folds into the COO** (the COO runs the breadth census every other run in a fresh session and drafts briefs; the Verification Analyst confirms each brief's feasibility, meaning the source answers and the expected treated count is readable within six months; the COO approves only CONFIRMED briefs, borderline ones go to the owner; the director's agent file and scoreboard stay as the method and its routine is retired); **the Internal Auditor merges into the Verification Analyst** (`fact-checker.md`: on-demand claim checks plus a scheduled audit of new research results, logged on branch `claude/research-audit`, run only when a research chat pushed new results; its only push is that log); the **release team** (Release Manager leads; Security Engineer reviews and fixes on request when the Release Manager writes "security review requested"; Site Accuracy Editor does a weekly Sunday deep read in a fresh session and reports to the Release Manager; the freshness gate runs on every pull request; the Release Manager keeps the trial log on branch `claude/release-log` and calls the Verification Analyst on any figure or "live" claim); the COO slows its own routine to every other day after three runs with nothing to decide and slows an idle research chat's check-in to every 6 hours; **research is backtest first** (`docs/CHARTER.md`: a data inventory, longer free history, one shared fast scan harness with the deflation count built in, survivors need profit factor 1.5 in three windows AND must hold up on data the scan never saw, at most three analysts at once). Not changed: the Security Engineer's weekly deep sweep and the Controller's Kalshi fee total wait for the owner's answers. Tests AB1 to AB6 in `test/agentBatchGates.test.js`. Docs and agent files only, live on merge.

## Efficiency across the board (Nov 7, 2026, the owner: "i don't want to be wasting tokens ... if we can operate more efficiently across the board ... a job for maybe the COO or the CFO")

`docs/CHARTER.md` now has eight efficiency rules every agent follows (read once; ask narrowly, one routine by `get_trigger` instead of the whole inventory; a fresh short session beats a big warm one, a chat near 780k tokens gets a fresh session and a short handoff; no helper agent for a three call job; short outputs; the lowest effort that works; stop at a closed source; batch independent calls). The COO (`ops-coo.md`, "Token efficiency") owns the token side and reports tokens per research verdict beside dollars, names the one habit costing the most and proposes one change; the Controller owns the dollar side. Test CH7 in `test/charterGates.test.js`. Docs and agent file only, live on merge.

## Edit layout strip no longer covers a folded card's title (Nov 7, 2026, the owner's screenshot)

In Edit layout the Drag handle, width, height and arrow buttons sat on top of the title of every folded card: a folded card loses all padding (`padding:0 !important`), and the strip is absolutely placed inside the card's top padding, so a 54px folded card had its title under the strip. While editing, a folded card now gets `padding-top` for the strip (56px, 64px on a phone). Reproduced headless first (folded card 54px tall, title at 1px, strip at 13px; after the fix the title starts at 57px, below the strip's 45px bottom). Test L15 in `test/layoutSmoothGates.test.js`. Page only, live on merge.

## Stops read cash, and OICORE's streak kill is 24 (Nov 7, 2026, the owner: "YES, raise the OICORE kill from 18 to 24 losses and have the bot's stops read fill-booked (real cash) P/L")

Why: the refinement chat found the bot's books worse than cash, because an order is booked at its limit price plus fee (`maxCost`) and fills come in better; and OICORE's 18-loss kill was likely a luck kill (a 10-loss streak at about 28% wins; 47% to 49% chance of 18 in a row within 100 fills at any size). **What changed:** `fillCost(t, got, want)` (`functions/kalshiLiveLib.js`, identical copy in `functions/kalshiSlotLib.js`, a test pins them equal) is (price of the side bought plus the per-contract fee) times the contracts that filled, from `averageFillPrice` (Kalshi gives it as the YES price, so a NO order pays 1 minus it) and `averageFeePaid`. Anything unreadable, or a cash figure above the limit booking, falls back to `maxCost` in proportion (fails closed). `settledFields` and `slotSettledFields` book a settle or an early sell at it, `botRisk` (the loss stop, L1's own stop, the shared stop) reads a settled order at it, and the slot's `accountOrder` (kill rules, own stop) likewise. **Not changed:** an order still OPEN stays at its worst case (`maxCost`) in every stop; the window cap's `spend` stays `maxCost`; sizing, the 12% stop, the 5.5% window cap, the 25c exit and the net kill (-$6 after 40 fills). **Effect to expect:** stops and kills trip a little later than before (they no longer overstate losses by the limit gap); a loss that hurts as before still counts in full. Orders settled before the deploy keep their old (limit-booked) `settledPnl`, so the nightly report and the page mix the two bases for a while. `OICORE.killRules.maxConsecutiveLosses` is 24 (the validator now allows up to 30). Tests FB1 to FB5 in `test/fillBookedGates.test.js`; the 18 and the old cap of 20 were updated in `kalshiSlotGates`, `kalshiSlotWiringGates`, `kalshiSlotIndexWiringGates`. Needs `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh` (the minute function) and `bash functions/deploy-push.sh` (the exit watch settles early sells), push first.

## OICORE skips entries under 8c (Nov 8, 2026, the owner approved designing small tests; the refinement chat's design W-DESIGN-OI, skip-only)

`SLOT_SKIP_BELOW = { OICORE: 0.08 }` and `slotSkipsPrice` in `functions/kalshiSlotLib.js`, one check in `functions/kalshiSlotRunLib.js` after the open interest test: when OICORE's signal passes every test but its cheap side is priced under 8c, no order is sent and the skip is logged as `cheap_skipped` (with the price) in `kalshiSlotSkips`. Why: live OICORE fills paid under 8c almost never won since Nov 1 and a forward group under 10c did not win at all, while the simulation says about break-even, so this is a bet that live beats the simulation; thin evidence, reversible. **It only removes orders** (max extra loss $0; the cost is forgone wins, about +$4.73 a 5-contract win against -$0.28 a loss, so 40 skipped signals net 5.0 x wins - $11). **Decision rule, written before the first skip:** about 4 signals a day, read at 40 skipped signals (about 10 days) from public settlements: keep the skip if at most 1 would have won, drop it (delete the table entry) if 4 or more would have, else read to 80. No new strategy tag (a second tagged entry would touch about 10 files and the "exactly two strategies" gates). It changes OICORE's own streak, kill and ladder inputs from the deploy day, so the deploy date is the start of the read. Tests in `test/kalshiSlotRunGates.test.js`. Needs `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh` only after the owner approves.

## In-game recorder (Nov 8, 2026, V2's INGAMELAG1 and SERIESLAG1 spec, the owner approved the build; research only)

`.github/workflows/record-ingame.yml` and `scripts/record_ingame.py`: every open NBA, NHL and NFL game's moneyline (both teams) and the two spread and two total markets nearest 50c at the first poll (fixed for the game) at about 20 seconds while a game is hot (a moneyline move of 2c or more starts three hot hours; about 2 minutes before), one public request per event, one stream with at least 0.5 s between calls and a doubling wait after a 429, at most 8 hot games at a time. Columns: receive time, the server's Date header, event, kind, ticker, strike, yes bid and ask, their sizes, last price, volume; a coverage row per game. Public endpoints only, no key, no order; the built-in token pushes gzipped CSV to the `sports-data` branch under `ingame/`, never to main (same push loop as the other recorders, test RECPATCH1 extended). Triggered every 15 minutes under one concurrency group that never cancels a running job, each job records up to the next 4 hour boundary; it stops by itself after 2027-01-31. NBA, NHL and NFL (the NHL and NFL series names, `KXNHLGAME`/`KXNHLSPREAD`/`KXNHLTOTAL` and `KXNFLGAME`/`KXNFLSPREAD`/`KXNFLTOTAL`, were verified by V2 from the history feed; a sport with no open games is simply skipped). It gives 20 second resolution that Kalshi's 1 minute candles cannot, for measuring lag between related markets. Tests IG1 to IG4 in `test/inGameRecorderGates.test.js`. Goes live on merge (it runs on GitHub's runners, not the bot); delete the workflow to stop it. GitHub runners use their own addresses, so it does not touch V2's sandbox read limit.

`skipSection` (`functions/kalshiReportLib.js`) adds `side` to each skip row: `"yes"` or `"no"` taken from the skip's note (price skips such as `cheap_skipped` store the side there), else null; free text is never echoed. The price was already in `detail.price`. Read only; no order, no write. Test K8 in `test/slotSkipReasonGates.test.js`. Needs `bash functions/deploy-report.sh` (the report function only).

## Shadow rules, the lifecycle's stage 1 (Nov 9, 2026, pull request (a) of `docs/STRATEGY_LIFECYCLE.md`)

*Superseded, see "State as of Nov 13, 2026" near the top (the shadow registry holds one rule, GOLDOFF; the rules named here are retired); kept as the record of what was decided then.*

`functions/kalshiShadowLib.js`: a shadow rule is a registered entry rule that only LOGS what it would have bought, with no money at risk. `SHADOW_RULES` is a frozen list in code and **starts empty** (a rule is a reviewed edit and a deploy, like the slot registry; nothing stored, requested or typed on the page can add one), so with no rule registered the deployed functions make exactly the reads, writes and orders they made before (the off-equivalence fixtures are untouched). `runShadowTick` runs in the minute function AFTER everything that can trade, only when the registry is not empty, on the quotes the bot already holds (the L1 markets: gold, Bitcoin, Ethereum, XRP), so a rule costs zero extra requests: no order, no Kalshi request, no key, one `create` per rule and market in `kalshiShadowRows` (admin read, no client write; the first in-band look of a window wins, so one decision a window is logged). The row carries the rule, ticker, side, price, size, the size resting at the touch, `wouldFill` (size at the touch covers the rule's size; a candle shadow overstates live, where the fill rate is about 65%), the quote and the seconds left. Nothing that decides an order reads the collection (gate SH4); the scorer (pull request (c)) will. Another market needs its quote read added in a reviewed edit. Tests SH1 to SH4 in `test/shadowLogGates.test.js`. Needs `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh` (the minute function) and, once, `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules` for the rule (the server writes without it).



## Week 1 shadow rules registered (Nov 9, 2026, pull request (e) of the lifecycle; the refinement chat's hunt)

*Superseded, see "State as of Nov 13, 2026" near the top (the shadow registry holds one rule, GOLDOFF; the rules named here are retired); kept as the record of what was decided then.*

`SHADOW_RULES` (`functions/kalshiShadowLib.js`) now holds four log only rules, pre-registered in `kalshi-scalper/README.md#SHADOW-WEEK1`: **ETHNO6** (Ethereum, NO side 0.88 to 0.97, 6 minute look), **BTCHI6** (Bitcoin, favourite 0.92 to 0.97, 6 minute look), **XRPYES10** (XRP, YES 0.88 to 0.97, 10 minute look), **ETHL1** (Ethereum, L1's entry); the lead figures are in the README pre-registration. Each is 1 contract and only logs: no order, no request, no money. **They are candidates, never passes** (N about 670 on top of about 540, no counterparty named, priors low). The first rows appear in `kalshiShadowRows` while a bot session is running, one per rule and market per window (the first in-band look wins), with `wouldFill` an upper bound (live fill rate is about 65%). Pass to an incubator: 150 forward signals, cash PF 1.3 or better, held out half 1.2 or better, 14 days, no day over 30% of net, then the owner's merge. Kill: net under -1c per contract after 50 signals, or under 40 signals in 30 days. Forward read 2026-12-14. Test SH5 pins the four rules. Needs the lifecycle (a) pull request merged first (it adds the logger), then `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh` (the minute function).

## The shadow scorer (Nov 9, 2026, pull request (c) of the lifecycle)

Shadow rows (`kalshiShadowRows`) are now SETTLED and SCORED. **Settlement:** every 5th minute the minute function, when a shadow rule is registered, reads at most 12 unsettled rows whose market closed 2 or more minutes ago and asks the PUBLIC market answer (`kalshiMarketApi().market`, no key, no order) for the result; the row gets `result`, `won`, `fee` and `pnl`, the dollars one contract would have made on the cash basis (the side's price plus the 10 contract fee: 7% of P x (1 - P) per contract, rounded up to the cent on ten contracts, as the research uses; `shadowFee`, `shadowOutcome`, `settleShadowRows`). Every error is swallowed; a row that cannot be read is tried again next time. It assumes a fill at the touch, an upper bound (`wouldFill` says whether the touch could cover the size). **Scoring:** the nightly report gains a `shadow` block (read only, last 90 days; `shadowReport`): per rule, signals, settled, fillable, wins, cash PF (all signals and fillable only), net cents per contract, second half PF, the top day's share of net, days, signals in the last 30 days, and a status against the written rules of `docs/STRATEGY_LIFECYCLE.md`: **kill** (net under -1c per contract after 50 fillable signals), **retire** (30 days old with under 40 signals in the last 30), **ready** (150 fillable signals, 14 days, PF 1.3 or better, second half 1.2 or better, no day over 30% of net, then the owner's promotion packet), else collecting. Numbers and rule names only, no ticker or id. The report library imports the scorer but still sends nothing to Kalshi and writes nothing; nothing that decides an order reads the log. Tests SC1 to SC3 in `test/shadowLogGates.test.js`. Needs `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh` (settlement) and `bash functions/deploy-report.sh` (the block); `deploy-latest.sh` picks both.

## Strategy lifecycle spec, draft (Nov 9, 2026, the owner: "constantly rotates strategies in and out by pre-set pass rules and kill rules")

`docs/STRATEGY_LIFECYCLE.md` (spec only, nothing live, no code): stages (research, shadow, incubator, live, retired), numeric pass rules and evidence sizes from the research chat (e.g. 400 fills for a cheap tail or 600 for a favourite, PF 1.5 on the held out half, a deflated z of 5.4 at N 1.5 million [superseded: ledger about 2.1 million tried, z 5.46 one-sided]; 4 promotions a quarter), kill rules calibrated by the refinement chat on gold and OICORE (net under -1c a contract after 50 fills, or rolling 60 fill PF under 0.7; no stage shorter than 14 days), and the code plan in four small pull requests (shadow logger and stage field; incubator cap and more kill rules; nightly scorer; Lifecycle table on the page). The line it keeps: automatic KILL inside caps is built and safe; PROMOTION stays a reviewed code edit the owner merges, because nothing stored may add or enlarge a strategy. A Charter change that would let the chief of staff merge a bounded class of promotions (shadow to incubator, 1 contract) is the owner's to make. Docs only; live on merge, nothing to deploy.

## GOLDALL shadow rule (Nov 9, 2026, the refinement chat's forward test of the gold momentum lead)

*Superseded, see "State as of Nov 13, 2026" near the top (the shadow registry holds one rule, GOLDOFF; the rules named here are retired); kept as the record of what was decided then.*

A fifth log only rule in `SHADOW_RULES` (`kalshi-scalper/README.md#SHADOW-GOLDALL`): gold, L1's own entry (favourite 0.88 to 0.97, 400 to 330 s before the close), 1 contract, logged for EVERY signal whether or not L1 orders. The lead: gold L1 orders placed after the bought side had already moved 5c or more in the last 3 minutes ("moving", `|rise3| >= 0.05`) did better than the rest in the record we have (refinement chat's cash replay: moving orders did far better than still ones, permutation p 0.013, but decaying between halves and **not deflated at N about 600**; the research chat's own figures differ and are being reconciled). rise3 logging only started on 2026-10-22, so older rows cannot test it. The row carries no rise3: it is computed at READ time from the public one minute candles with `riseFromCandles`; the split is pre-registered in the README and the same rows give the unfiltered baseline. Pass: 150 forward signals (at least 50 moving), moving cash PF 1.3 or better, second half 1.2 or better, 14 days. Kill: moving net under -1c per contract after 50 moving signals. Forward read 2026-12-14. Log only, no new code path. Test SH5 pins the five rules. Needs `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh` (the minute function).

A fourth page, `id="page-strategies"`, with its own nav entry (`data-page="strategies"`, hidden until `applyAdminUI` shows it), admin only and login gated the same way as the Bot page (`showPage` refuses it for anyone else and `applyAdminUI` sends a non-admin away from it). Phone first, then desktop: a **Lifecycle** card (Shadow, Live, Retired as three stages with arrows, a count and the strategies as chips; at most four names on a stage, then "+N more"), then one tile per strategy under Live and On deck (a collapsed Retired line at the bottom, as in the card). A tile: the cash profit factor big and first (30 days for live, since the log began for a shadow rule), a trend line (`kalshiStratRolling`: rolling 30 trade cash PF for a live strategy from the orders the page holds; `kalshiStratCumulative`: cumulative cash PF by signal for a shadow rule, from the 8th signal on; both capped at 3 and drawn against a dashed 1.0 line), a half circle kill gauge for live strategies (`kalshiStratGauge`: OICORE the drawdown used of its $50 stop with "drawdown room $X of $50", the gold tripwire as n of 100 trades, EVENT830 the losing streak of 20), signals of 150 as a bar for each shadow rule, a **Sizing ladder** row of static labels (`KAL_BOARD_LADDER`, the owner's decision of Nov 10, 2026: new probes start at 3 contracts inside today's caps, no Charter change, stepping up per the ladder rules with the $20 hard cap, the per-contract kill and a step down on a kill; gold 'Balance ladder, held at 15', OICORE 'Own ladder, step 10 (10, 12, 15, 20, 30)', EVENT830 '1 contract', XRPYES10 '1 contract' because the owner said it stays at 1, every other shadow rule 'Next: 3 contracts as a probe'). The labels are text only; the bot's code decides every size, and the Implement, Kill and Return block (`KAL_BOARD_LIVE`, `KAL_BOARD_SHADOW_RULES`) in an expandable Rules line. It reads only what the Bot page already reads (the orders listeners start at admin sign in; the shadow log once per page load, kept 10 minutes, never on a timer, `kalshiShadowEnsure`), writes nothing and calls no new host. Every dollar goes through `kalshiMoney`, so Privacy view masks it (the page has its own Privacy button, `stratPresentBtn`, the same switch as the Bot page's); profit factors and counts are never masked. Black and white only, no green or red. The pure functions of the old card (`kalshiBoard*`) are kept and their tests SB1 to SB13 still hold (SB10 and SB12 now describe the page); the new visuals' functions are tested in `test/strategiesPageGates.test.js`. XRPYES10 was admitted to the second slot on Nov 10, 2026, so it now has a Live tile (`KAL_BOARD_LIVE`, kill: 3 losses in a row, shown as a gauge, 3 contracts held to settlement) AND keeps its Shadow tile (the shadow rule still logs; the Details of the two are kept apart by a `Live` or `Shadow` key prefix). No Settings landing choice was added for the page. Page only, live on merge; nothing to deploy.

**Summary bar, detail panels and timeline (Nov 10, 2026, the owner via the assistant: more informational; an evidence or odds panel is explicitly NOT wanted).** (1) A **summary bar** above the Lifecycle (`kalshiStratSummary`, `kalshiStratSummaryHtml`): the blended cash profit factor over 30 days for every strategy, big and first, then net cash since the start, today (Central day), the last 7 days, **at risk now** (the `openCost` of `kalshiRiskMeter`, open orders at their worst case, the same figure the Stop Loss meter uses) and the number of open positions (`kalshiStratOpenCount`: filled, not settled, not sold out). All strategies' orders from `kalshiAllOrders()`. (2) A **Details** button under each tile (a `<details class="sp-det" data-k="ID">`; which ones are open is kept in `kalshiStratOpen` and restored on each redraw by a `toggle` listener on `#stratBody`, because the page redraws on every feed update). Live strategies (`kalshiStratDetail`): a cumulative cash result line (inline SVG, dashed zero line), win rate, average win, average loss, fees paid (stored `averageFeePaid` times contracts filled, where stored), best and worst day (Central) and the last 8 settled trades (opened time, market, side, cash result). Shadow rules (`kalshiStratShadowDetail`): signals, win rate, average win and loss in cents a contract, best and worst day. (3) A **Timeline** and **Recent activity** at the bottom: `KAL_STRAT_TIMELINE` is a fixed dated list in code (gold tripwire from Nov 9 with its own n of 100, OICORE forward read 2026-11-13, XRPFAV6 shadow data 2026-11-24, the Dec 9 checkpoint, XRPYES10 first forward read and shadow weeks 1 and 2 reads 2026-12-14; a reviewed edit, never computed); Recent activity reuses `kalshiNotifFeed` (the Bot page's notification items, no new read) filtered to critical, warning, session started and "Bot stopped by you" (`kalshiStratActivity`), or says "No kills or promotions recorded." No new host, listener on data, timer or write; dollars through `kalshiMoney` (Privacy masks them), every value escaped, black and white only. Tests SP9 to SP14 in `test/strategiesPageGates.test.js`.

## Strategies page animation (Nov 10, 2026, the owner: animated visuals: gauges that fill, profit factor figures that tick up, chips that flow through the lifecycle, lines that draw in)

This is the owner's explicit, narrow exception to "Do not add a count-up or any animation on a figure" (Bubble pop, above): it covers ONLY the cash profit factor figures on the Strategies page (`data-pf`, the summary bar's and each tile's). Nothing on the Bot page, Brief or Settings animates a figure, and the owner did not want a scoreboard, colour glow or live ticker; it stays black and white. Played once each time the page is shown (`showPage('strategies')` sets `kalshiStratAnim.pending`; `renderStrategiesPage` starts it when the shadow data has settled and a redraw never replays it: a redraw stops any play and leaves the markup at rest). Parts: the kill gauge arc and the sparklines draw by `stroke-dashoffset` (`pathLength="1"`, reusing `lineDraw`), the signals bars and the lifecycle chips and arrows move by `transform` and `opacity` under `#page-strategies.sp-play` (JS adds the class for 1.9 s), and the figures count from 0.00 to the true value over 700 ms with ease out cubic (`kalshiStratEase`, `kalshiStratCountText`; one `requestAnimationFrame` loop plus one timeout, no interval, no storage, no network). The true value is always in the markup, set again when the loop ends or is cut off, and in `aria-label`; "n/a" and "no losses" never animate. Under `prefers-reduced-motion` or `data-reduce-motion` nothing starts and the true values show at once (the CSS rules are also in both reduce-motion blocks, test G1). The JS is fenced by `STRAT-ANIM-BEGIN` and `STRAT-ANIM-END` markers in `index.html`; `test/bubblePopGates.test.js` P2 was edited ON PURPOSE to skip that block and still forbids a count-up anywhere else, and `test/strategiesAnimationGates.test.js` SA1 to SA12 pins the scope. A feed redraw landing inside the 700 ms cuts the play short (it shows the true values at once). Page only, live on merge.

## Strategies flow board, polish round 1 (Nov 10, 2026, the owner, with a generic layout mock-up as reference only)

*Superseded, see "State as of Nov 13, 2026" near the top (the current design is one Live box with Trial and Proven tags and the Wheel view); kept as the record of what was decided then.*

Lanes are now **Live, Shadow (on deck), Retired**, left to right on a desktop and top to bottom on a phone; the cards inside Live and Shadow put a strategy that has a twin in the other lane first (`kalshiStratTwinFirst`; today XRPYES10), so the side link is short. **Lines:** `kalshiFlowLinks` now returns at most one SIDE link per twin (shadow card left edge to its live twin's right edge, `kalshiFlowPath`) and ONE trunk (`kalshiFlowTrunk`: up out of the Live header, across 12px above the headers, down into the Retired header) instead of a curve per retired strategy. **Cards:** the first element is a ring dial (`kalshiStratRing`: a circle with the cash profit factor inside, still the `data-pf` figure that counts up, an arc of `pf / 2` of the ring, `no losses` full, `n/a` empty, a tick at 1.0 at the bottom); live cards show the kill gauge as a bar (the half circle and `kalshiStratArc` are gone), shadow cards are compact (a smaller ring, name, badge and signals bar; their rule and status sit inside Details). **Details** (the same `sp-det` fold, same open state kept) now holds the trend line, the sizing ladder, Implement, Kill and Return as a plain list (`kal-bd-rules`, no nested fold) and the evidence panel. **Retired** is a compact list, one line per strategy (name, note, PF and n), folded behind its header by default on a phone AND a desktop (the checkbox state is kept). Page only, live on merge.

## OKX liquidation recorder (Nov 10, 2026, the owner approved; research only)

`.github/workflows/record-oklq.yml` and `scripts/record_oklq.py` record OKX's public liquidation feed for the Bitcoin perpetual swaps (BTC-USDT-SWAP and BTC-USD-SWAP): time, side, size and bankruptcy price of each filled liquidation, for the research question of whether a liquidation cascade moves Bitcoin's 15 minute market. Public OKX endpoint, no key, no auth header, no order; the built-in token pushes gzipped CSV to the `quote-data` branch under `oklq/`, never to main. 4 starts a day (crons `7 0,12` and `7 3,15`, gaps of 3 h and 9 h; a dropped start leaves at most 12 h), about 120 billed minutes a month, about 120 to 170 GETs a day. It stops by itself: from 2026-12-31 23:59 UTC every run exits at once; the first read is about early January 2027. **Caveat:** OKX keeps only about 17 hours of this feed, so it can only be logged forward, never fetched later. **Check in the first week** the largest gap between `t_start_ms` values in `coverage.csv`: it must stay under about 12 hours. Scheduled workflows run only from main, so nothing records until this is merged. Tests OL1 to OL7 in `test/oklqRecorderGates.test.js`. Goes live on merge; stop it sooner by deleting the workflow.


## Qualified-but-no-order record (P3) and decision-time book snapshot (P2) (Nov 11, 2026, the owner: "P3 + P2", both LOG ONLY)

**P3.** A slot skip record (`kalshiSlotSkips/{strategy}_{ticker}`, written after the minute's orders by `slotSkipWriter`) that happens AFTER a strategy's open interest condition passed now also carries `qualified: true`, `oiRise` (the rise that passed), `side` and `price` (the side and price it would have bought), so every trigger can be scored whether or not an order went out; the skip reasons themselves (`shard_short`, `cap`, `direction_lock`, `loss_stop`, `stale_tick`, `cheap_skipped`, `halted`, `record_failed`, `already_attempted`, `scale_in`, `position_unreadable`, flow codes) are unchanged. A market that never triggered (`oi_below`, `no_oi_candle`, a price outside the band, a whole-tick skip such as `no_shared_risk`) gets none of the new fields and no new row is made. Only strategies with an open interest condition (OICORE) qualify this way. `skipSection` in the nightly report exposes `qualified` (true or null), `oiRise`, `price` as numbers and `side` as `yes` or `no` only, never free text or ids. **P2.** After every order of the minute has been sent (L1 in `runL1Tick`, the slot at the end of `runSlotTick`), one public order book read per order the exchange answered (`logBook`, `GET /markets/{ticker}/orderbook`, no key, no order, a 1.5 second abort, `BOOK_READ_TIMEOUT_MS`, and NO 429 retry: a refusal simply writes nothing) is written onto that order as `bookYes5` and `bookNo5` (the best 5 YES and 5 NO bids, best first, each `{p: dollars, q: size}`; Firestore refuses an array inside an array) and `bookReadMs` (wall clock of the read). The reads of a minute run in parallel and the whole snapshot (reads and the Firestore writes) is cut off after 1.8 seconds from when it starts (`boundBookWrites`, `BOOK_TOTAL_MS`, in both engines; it uses `AbortSignal.timeout`, so the live module still holds exactly one wait, gate L10), so it can never delay a tick's return, and so the next entry, by more than 1.8 s (order latency: a late send fills worse); a write still running after the cut-off finishes or fails on its own and its error is swallowed. A failed, refused, empty or slow read leaves the order exactly as it was and every error is swallowed; no read on a minute with no order and none for an order whose answer was an error. Copy all orders and Copy order diagnostics carry `bookYes5`, `bookNo5` (as `price:size|price:size`) and `bookReadMs`; the nightly report rows (`?orders=1`) carry them as `[price, size]` pairs and `bookReadMs`. **Nothing that decides an order reads any of it** (gate BK8: the deciding files never name the fields), no change to size, price, limit, timing, sequence, shard or exit, no new secret, endpoint or callable, `firestore.rules` unchanged. **Off-equivalence fixture, re-recorded on purpose (the Oct 31 send-time precedent):** `offEquivalence.json` now holds the old record plus exactly the new `GET .../orderbook` fetch entries (5 scenarios that send an order); the old record is kept as `offEquivalencePreBook.json`, and BK12 requires the new one, with those orderbook GETs stripped, to equal it byte for byte (`bookReadMs` is masked like `sentAtMs` in the scenario runner). `offEquivalenceIndex.json` is untouched. **What they cannot do:** P2 is one top-5 snapshot read AFTER the order, so it shows the book a few hundred milliseconds to seconds after the fill, not the book the decision saw (the decision-time touch sizes are `seen` and `depthAtLimit`); a missed snapshot is a null, never a guess. P3 says only that the trigger passed and why no order followed; it does not say the order would have won. Tests BK1 to BK16 in `test/logP3P2Gates.test.js` (BK13 to BK16: a read that never answers, a 429, the constants). **Deploy:** `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh` (the minute function) then `bash functions/deploy-report.sh` (the report), or `bash functions/deploy-latest.sh`.

## External-signal shadow logger (Nov 11, 2026, the owner approved; research only)

Owner approval (popup, Nov 11, 2026): "EXTERNAL-SIGNAL SHADOW LOGGER: APPROVED". `.github/workflows/record-signals.yml` and `scripts/record_signals.py` log any REGISTERED public signal together with the Kalshi quote at that moment, so unique non-price shadow candidates (weather, scores, options, onchain) can be tested forward without a new recorder each time. It places no order, reads no key, writes nothing to Firebase and never touches `functions/`. **The registry** is `kalshi-scalper/signals/REGISTRY.json` (committed, reviewed edits only): each entry has an id (2 to 12 capitals or digits), a readme pointer to its pre-registration, an https url whose host must be on the script's hard ALLOWLIST (the hosts the other recorders already read, plus api.weather.gov, www.okx.com, www.deribit.com, api.coinbase.com, api.kraken.com; api.binance.com is NOT allowed; adding a host is a code edit), a declarative parse spec (a JSON path or a CSV column and row, no eval, no regex, a number or a string of at most 40 characters), one Kalshi ticker or series (public `GET /markets` only: bid, ask, sizes, close time), a cadence (`run` = every start, `day` = first start of the UTC day) and a stopDate (not later than 2027-02-28). An entry records nothing unless `enabled` is true; the shipped registry has one example entry, off, so **merging records nothing**. **Politeness and bounds:** stdlib only, a polite User-Agent with no personal details, at least 1 second between requests, a 429 or 5xx backs off, at most 24 requests, 8 entries and 256 KB a response per run, a 40 second budget, one failing source never stops the next, it always exits 0. **Runner minutes (the Charter's 250 a month):** 4 starts a day (`11 1,7,13,19`), about 1 billed minute each (checkout and a 40 second request budget; GitHub bills whole minutes), so 4 x 31 days x 1 minute = **124 minutes a month at most**; with the OKX recorder (about 120) the two together are about 244, under the 250 limit; even at 2 billed minutes a start it would be 248 for this job alone, which is why the time budget exists. Sports score hosts (ESPN, NHL, MLB, NBA.com) are deliberately not on the host allowlist: their terms forbid automated collection. With the OKX recorder's about 120 (its own figure, not measured here) that is about 244 for the two, so check the month's usage in Settings, Billing before enabling a real entry. **Output:** gzipped CSV to the `quote-data` branch under `signals/<date>/<HHMM>.csv.gz` (columns: id, signal value and read time and status and server Date header, Kalshi ticker, bid, ask, sizes, close time, quote read time and Date header, coverage flags), pushed with the six-try loop, never main. It stops itself at 2027-02-28 23:59 UTC (workflow and script) and at each entry's stopDate. **How a candidate is added:** a reviewed pull request that edits the registry AND carries its pre-registration and the four uniqueness checks of `docs/STRATEGY_LIFECYCLE.md` section 7d (which may arrive in another open PR), then the owner's approval to set `enabled`. Nothing to deploy; live on merge but records nothing until an entry is enabled. Tests SG1 to SG8 in `test/signalLoggerGates.test.js`.

## Favourite-late duplicates retired (Nov 11, 2026, the owner: "existing duplicates should be killed. Why are we running multiple of the same strategies?")

The owner, disappointed that the duplicates got this far, retired every copy of the favourite-late mechanism except gold L1, which is the single favourite-late strategy. **Removed:** the live second-slot probes `XRPYES10` (XRP, L1's entry at a 10 minute look) and `KESTREL` (AUD/USD) from `SLOT_STRATEGIES`, `SLOT_HOLD_ALLOWED` (back to OICORE and EVENT830), `SLOT_SERIES_ALLOWED` (`KXXRP15M`, `KXAUDUSD15M`), `SLOT_SERIES_RESERVED` (KESTREL's AUD/USD) and `SLOT_TICKER_RE` (both tickers), and `slotRollingSeriesDue` with its line in `functions/index.js` (no strategy needs the rolling quote read now; the minute function reads no AUD/USD market for the slot); and all four shadow rules (`XRPYES10`, `GOLDALL`, `XRPFAV6`, `LANTERN`) from `SHADOW_RULES`, so the registry is EMPTY and `runShadowTick` returns `skipped: "no shadow rule"` with no write (the minute function does not call it, or the settle sweep, while the registry is empty; test SH5). **Kept, untouched:** gold L1 (sizes, caps, the shared 12% stop, the 25c exit, the gold kill), OICORE and EVENT830. XRP, Bitcoin, Ethereum and the probe markets stay paused for L1 (a separate decision).

**What happens to an order XRPYES10 or KESTREL already holds (investigated, and proved by `RET1` to `RET3` in `test/kalshiSlotRunGates.test.js` through the real engine and the real registry).** Nothing is stranded, but it is NOT held to settlement any more. `runSlotExits` takes every unsettled filled slot order in the collection (not only registered strategies) and looks its rules up by `slotId`; with the strategy gone it gets `FALLBACK_EXIT` (stop at a bid of 25c, and always out 60 seconds before the close). So: a healthy favourite with time left is held; at a bid of 25c or less it is sold (`exitWhy: "stop"`); 60 seconds before the close it is sold at the bid (`exitWhy: "time"`), instead of riding to the settle as the strategy would have; a position that closed before either is booked by `runSlotSettle`, and `SLOT_EVER_USED` keeps `slotWatch()` true so the exit pass, the settle sweep and the exit loop stay awake (`slotExitIdle` is false while it is open). It keeps counting as open money in the shared 12% stop (`slotRiskForBotRisk`) and the 5.5% window cap (`slotWindowSpent`) until settled or sold, because both key on `strategy: "SLOT"` and not on the registry, and a settled one stays in the record. A removed strategy has no kill latch to write (the exit code skips it when the strategy is not found). The exit watch's sell goes through the same per-market `claimClose` lease as Flatten, and the hedge rule still applies. If the exit watch deployed is an OLDER revision it already does this (the fallback exists since the slot was built; the README's deploy-order note for XRPYES10 and KESTREL describes exactly this behaviour), so there is no window in which an open order is unmanaged, and the worst case is a 25c fallback sell instead of a hold. Only one thing is lost: the hold-to-settlement edge on an order still open at the deploy (at most about 10 minutes of exposure at 3 contracts, about $2.9 each).

**Page.** `KAL_SLOT_REGISTERED` is OICORE and EVENT830; the two retired ids keep a label "(retired)" in `KAL_STRATEGY_INFO`, and the Strategies table shows a removed strategy only while it has an open order (the FLOW1 precedent, test ST17). The Own stops line is `OICORE + EVENT830`; the Rules text and the Start confirmation name only gold L1, OICORE and EVENT830 (the XRP and AUD/USD sentences are gone); the kill gauge, ladder labels and live cards of the two are gone and the timeline lost the XRPFAV6, XRPYES10, KESTREL and shadow week reads. `KAL_BOARD_SHADOW` is empty and the Shadow lane says "None running". `KAL_BOARD_RETIRED` (as of Nov 11) lists XRPYES10, KESTREL, GOLDALL, XRPFAV6 and LANTERN with NO figures and one line each carrying its written return rule from `kalshi-scalper/README.md`: the live probes and the shadow rules return after a 14 day cool off and a fresh shadow run at the shadow bar (XRPYES10: 100 fills at cash PF 1.3+, second half 1.2+, owner approval; KESTREL: a forward read at cash PF 1.3+ over 40 fills, owner approval; GOLDALL and XRPFAV6: 150 signals, cash PF 1.3+, second half 1.2+; LANTERN: the owner's Nov 10 rule, live only at cash PF 1.3+ over 150 forward signals, second half 1.2+, at least 14 days). Each README section carries a dated "Retired Nov 11, 2026" note with the owner's words. **A gap to decide:** the gold kill latch's written re-arm evidence (`docs/GOLD_KILL_LATCH.md`, `docs/STRATEGY_LIFECYCLE.md` section 7) names the GOLDALL shadow rows; they no longer accrue, so a gold re-arm needs a fresh GOLDALL run or another source the owner names (both docs now say so). `docs/STRATEGY_LIFECYCLE.md` section 7c: the favourite-late shadow count is 0; the rule stays (a new favourite-late rule needs a stated distinct reason).

**Tests.** `RET1` to `RET3` (open retired orders are held above 25c, sold at 25c or 60 s before the close, settled by the sweep, counted in the stop and the window cap, and no new entry of either can be sent, through the real tick), `XR1` to `XR4` and `AU1` to `AU4` (rewritten as "gone from every list, cannot enter, README note and return rule"), `SH5` (empty registry behaves like the old empty one), `RT1` (the board). The registry counts in the slot gates, SP3, SP5, SP12, FB4, SB7, SB11, SB13, SF3, FL5 and OM1 were updated on purpose; the board render tests use a fixture of four shadow rules because the renderer is kept for the next real one. The off-equivalence fixtures are untouched and byte identical (they are baked with the slot registry empty or forced off). Needs, IN THIS ORDER: `bash functions/deploy-push.sh` first (`kalshiLiveExit`, the exit watch, and `kalshiFlattenAll` carry the slot library; this change removes no exit code, so an older exit watch behaves the same for an open order, but the redeploy makes both carry the new registry), then `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh` (`kalshiLiveArmed`, the minute function: the registry, the empty shadow tick and no AUD/USD read; the script also redeploys `kalshiBooks`, `kalshiLiveAccount` and `kalshiGoldKillCheck` unchanged). Optional: `bash functions/deploy-report.sh` (until it is redeployed the nightly report keeps scoring the old four shadow rules from the rows already logged). The page change goes live on merge.

## R&D operating charter (Nov 11, 2026, the owner: "i think we need to define stuff for the R&D team otherwise they're going to get carried away"; in force)

`docs/RND_CHARTER.md` is in force (the owner approved it between Nov 11 and Nov 12, 2026) and binds the research chat (V2) and the live-performance chat (Refinement): a one line mission, what is in and out of scope, an intake gate (the four uniqueness checks of `docs/STRATEGY_LIFECYCLE.md` section 7d plus a one line registration with the bar), limits (3 concurrent tests, one test per family, shadow caps, usage pacing, what counts as FALSIFIED and NOT_ENOUGH_DATA), the Monday outputs, a definition of done for a test, escalation, and a table of what "carried away" looks like with the automatic stop for each. **The owner's four popup decisions of Nov 11, 2026 are written in (the concurrency one is now: 2 concurrent tests, or the Head of R&D's current worker rule):** (1) pace, "5 must make it onto the shadow list per week", meaning five unique candidates that pass the four checks and the declined list, counted only when pre-registered, already collecting on a named source and given a read date and N (a short week is reported as short, never padded; the shadow registry carries only Kalshi price rules on four markets, so most are recorder-backed or read-time joins); (2) 2 concurrent tests at a time, not 3 (or the Head of R&D's current worker rule); (3) 3 falsified pre-registered tests put a mechanism family on the declined list for good (two NOT_ENOUGH_DATA from one source still close that source); (4) an owner popup is required for any strategy going live, even a 1 contract probe, any exception to a ban or rule, and any spend or new recorder over the 250 runner minute limit. The numbers are listed in its last line for the owner to change. Later the same day the owner also decided: Kalshi's own data is a different source only if it is a different field (not the same price band at another time); the Verification Analyst scores the four checks and the owner approves each shadow entry; a correlation of 0.3 or more is a rejected duplicate on the declined list with the number. It does not edit `docs/CHARTER.md`, which only the owner changes. Docs only, nothing to deploy. Also decided: a paired exit means the same strategy's own legs only, and a replacement for gold L1 (now a Probe under review, still trading under its kill) is the top priority both Monday reports lead with.

## The uniqueness check (Nov 11, 2026, the owner: "how many times have i told you to continue finding unique strategies?")

A HARD RULE, docs only (`docs/STRATEGY_LIFECYCLE.md` section 7d). Every new candidate states its entry, management and exit and names the closest existing mechanism and why it differs, before it gets a shadow slot or test time. A favourite-late variant (same 88c to 97c late-window logic, different market, look time or size) is rejected as a duplicate unless the owner approves. The backlog is ranked by uniqueness first, then prior; the weekly rotation review shows each strategy's mechanism family; V2 and Refinement each propose five genuinely different mechanisms; "menu empty" is never an answer (say what data or venue would be needed and the cost). Today's families: favourite-late (live and shadow), cheap-side open interest (OICORE), event or calendar (EVENT830); every live strategy except gold holds to settlement, so there is no exit or management diversity yet. Live on merge; nothing to deploy. Decided the same day (lifecycle 7d to 7g): a paired exit means the same strategy's own legs only, never a hedge against another strategy or market; the account-level 12% stop and 5.5% window cap are the account's safety net, not strategy interference; gold L1 is labelled Probe (under review) and keeps trading under its existing kill, and a replacement for gold is the top research priority, reported first each week.

## Liquidity provision counts as unique (Nov 11, 2026, the owner: "a no-signal LIQUIDITY-PROVISION family counts as unique")

Docs only, nothing to deploy. `docs/STRATEGY_LIFECYCLE.md` 7f rule 6, 7d check 1 and `docs/RND_CHARTER.md` section 3: a market-making family (P&L from the spread and queue position, time-flatten exit) has its signal check NOT APPLICABLE and is judged by entry mechanism and exit style. It still needs correlation under 0.3 against every live strategy, off the declined list, cash PF 1.3 (100 fills, 2 of 3 windows), and its own owner popup before anything goes live. MMSIM1 is added to the 7f table as an Idea, simulation only.

## The declined list (Nov 11, 2026, the owner: "we have multiple duplicates that I hard killed and said NO to very clearly and somehow they made it to live?")

`docs/STRATEGY_LIFECYCLE.md` section 7e records every idea the owner declined or evidence closed, with the names each must not return under. Every candidate, shadow slot, owner popup and strategy pull request is checked against it first and the check goes in the uniqueness note; a renamed or near-identical version counts as the declined idea; a popup asking for an exception must quote the earlier NO; the Release Manager and the weekly rotation review (7b) check it; only the owner removes an entry. A Charter line is proposed for the owner (the Charter is the owner's to change). Docs only, nothing to deploy. Section 7f (Nov 11, 2026) classifies strategies: family = entry mechanism plus exit style, a risk class, five stages (Idea, Shadow, Probe, Live, Retired) and a proven bar of cash PF 1.3 or better over 100 fills in 2 of 3 windows (definition proposed); every registration carries a one-line classification card. Section 7g (Nov 11, 2026) says a filter, improvement or parameter tweak (including the same logic on a new market) is a VERSION that replaces its parent (v2 replaces v1, v1 retired with its record), never a new strategy, never unique, never a shadow slot or part of the cap; it is tested in shadow beside the parent and takes over only if its cash PF beats the parent's by 0.10, its net dollars per contract-week are not lower, its second half is not worse and it has 60 signals. Two strategies may share an underlying market if signal and entry differ, with no opposite sides, one shared cap and stop, and each selling only its own contracts. A regime-only strategy is its own strategy with a regime tag, off when its signal is missing. Owner popup answers (Nov 11, 2026, docs only): interference means opposite sides of one market or a shared loss cap or stop (stacking and book depth do not count; proposed wording A makes each strategy need its own stop and window budget before sharing a market, full isolation B is an open question, owner popup pending); Kalshi's own data is a different source only if it is a different field with different logic; the independent Verification Analyst scores the four checks and the owner approves each shadow entry; a correlation of 0.3 or more is a rejected duplicate recorded on the declined list with the number; GOLDHOLD (gold held to settlement) is on the declined list.

The Polymarket lead filter on OICORE in `docs/STRATEGY_LIFECYCLE.md` is parked with it. **The gate:** `test/noSportsScoreSitesGates.test.js` (NS1 to NS3) now also bans every `*.polymarket.com` host in workflows, scripts and functions (comments included, so do not name a host there), NS3 and SG1 keep it off the signal logger allowlist, and R4 in `test/refRecorderGates.test.js` allows only the Kalshi host in the recorder code. **How to reverse it:** a decision by the owner or counsel plus written permission from Polymarket, then a reviewed edit of the gates and the recorder; nothing here resumes on a date.

## The non-crypto quote read, NC15 (Nov 11, 2026, the owner: "QUOTE READ on the bot's non-crypto markets"; research only, nothing trades)

`.github/workflows/record-nc-book.yml` and `scripts/record_nc_book.py` are R6 (the 15 minute book recorder above) pointed at the bot's NON-crypto 15 minute series: `KXSILVER15M`, `KXCOPPER15M`, `KXWTI15M`, `KXEURUSD15M`, `KXGBPUSD15M`, `KXUSDJPY15M`, `KXHYPE15M`, `KXBNB15M`, `KXAUDUSD15M`, `KXUSDCAD15M`, `KXNATGAS15M` (fixed in code; gold and the five crypto series are R6's, not repeated). Why: the research chats want a quote read for the non-crypto markets for their mass backtest sweep and for spread, depth and timing tests. Every 5 seconds (it was 2 seconds in the first draft; changed so the data branch stays small) one batched public book read (`/markets/orderbooks`, falling back to one read per market after 3 failed batches in a row) keeps the best 5 YES and 5 NO bids with sizes, the server Date header and the send and receive time; about 60 seconds after each close every public trade of that window (cursor followed to the end, de-duplicated by trade id, id not stored); a coverage row per window per market; an hourly SNTP clock check. Public Kalshi endpoints only: no key, no auth header, no order, no Firestore write, nothing under `functions/` touched, nothing deployed. Two request streams, at least 0.5 s between book calls and 1.5 s between tape pages, a 429 waits 2 s doubling to 30. The built-in token pushes gzipped CSV to its OWN `nc-data` branch (created as an orphan branch by the first run) under `nc15/<date>/<HHMM>/`, never main and never `quote-data` (which is already about 726 MB, and every job fetches its whole branch). Triggered every 15 minutes (`5,35` and `20,50`, as R6 after Oct 31, so a dropped start costs at most 15 minutes) under one concurrency group that never cancels a running job; each job records up to the next 4 hour boundary. **It stops by itself on 2026-12-31 23:59 UTC** (checked in the workflow and in the script; a later date is a reviewed edit); stop it sooner by deleting the workflow. **Estimates (not measured yet):** at the 5 second poll one batched read is 12 a minute, about 14.5 a minute on average with the housekeeping reads (discovery, results, tape), and about 50 at the peak minute after a close; about 20 to 36 MB a day gzipped (R6 measured about 36 MB a day for 5 markets at 2 seconds; 11 markets at 5 seconds is about 190,000 book rows a day when all are open, fewer at weekends when forex and metals close; the tape is small), so about 1 to 1.8 GB by Dec 31 on its OWN branch, which a git branch can carry (the first draft, every 2 seconds to Feb 28, would have been about 5 GB). **Discovery** asks the 11 series one at a time, so the last market can be found about 16 seconds after a window opens: the first seconds of its book may be missing, and that is not flagged in the coverage row. **Runner minutes:** it is continuous like R6, one job running at all times, about 43,000 billed minutes a month. That is free on a public repo, but it is far over the Charter's 250 minute guideline; the owner's merge accepts that figure. Tests NC15 gates FB1 to FB12 in `test/ncBookRecorderGates.test.js` (fixed series list, public host only, redirects refused FB12, no key, nc15 folder on nc-data only, never quote-data, orphan creation of the branch, six-try push loop, no-cancel concurrency, minimal permissions, pinned checkout, the stop date). `record-15m-book` and R6's gates are unchanged.

## Market strip and Brief moved off Yahoo (Nov 11, 2026, the owner)

*Superseded Nov 13, 2026: this move was reverted on Nov 12, 2026 (see "Market strip back on Yahoo: an owner-accepted terms risk"); the Brief and strip read Yahoo again. Kept as the record of the permitted-source scoping.*

**Why.** Yahoo's terms forbid automated access and building databases (legal.yahoo.com, 2(d)(9), 2(e), 2(d)(10)), and nobody had read the terms of the news feeds or CNN's page. The owner chose a permitted set with no spend and an honest gap for the rest (`docs/MARKET_TAPE_SOURCES.md`, section "Nov 11 confirmation"). **The permitted set:** Treasury's daily par yield curve for the 10 and 30 year yields (a once a day close, shown as "close of <date>"; the endpoint was confirmed to answer, the terms page has no ban and no explicit grant, so re-read it) and alternative.me's crypto fear and greed (commercial use allowed with the credit "Crypto fear and greed index data from alternative.me" shown directly under the reading). **What now says "No price":** S&P 500, Nasdaq 100, Dow, Russell 2000, VIX, gold, crude oil, Bitcoin, Ethereum, SPY, QQQ and DIA, in the strip and the Brief; the Brief's three chart pairs say "No chart: no permitted source yet"; Market News says "No news source yet". The strip's "Long bonds" (TLT) became the 30Y yield. **Dropped:** CNN's stocks fear and greed reading and every RSS feed (CNBC, MarketWatch, WSJ, CoinDesk, Cointelegraph); the page also stopped using `shared/marketSnapshot` headlines as a fallback, and shows headlines only when the tape document carries a `newsSource` marker, which nothing writes. **How it works:** `marketTapeTick` runs hourly (the sources change daily), writes the whole document with `buildTapeDoc` (a failed source keeps its older close with its own date; unsourced symbols are `noPrice: true`; nothing a removed source wrote survives), and the page ignores any quote with no `source` or marked `noPrice` (`tapeUsableQuotes`), so an old Yahoo quote still in the document is never shown. **To add a permitted crypto source** (Bitcoin and Ethereum wait for the owner to read the exchange's market data terms): add one reader to `READERS` and its host to `ALLOWED_HOSTS` in `marketTapeLib.js`, set that symbol's `source` in `SYMBOLS`, and add the host to the exact list in `test/noYahooMarketTapeGates.test.js`; a symbol with no source renders No price. Tests: `test/marketTapeGates.test.js` (T1 to T8), `test/noYahooMarketTapeGates.test.js` (Y1 to Y5, reconcile with the research no-Yahoo gate if one lands on main). **Deploy:** needs `npx firebase-tools deploy --only functions:marketTapeTick --project shwoopnet` by the owner (keyless, no switch); the page change goes live on merge. Either order is safe: page first shows No price for every old Yahoo quote (it has no `source`) and no headlines, then the deploy writes the new document; function first writes the new document and the old page shows dashes. Deploy right after the merge, because until then Yahoo is still being read. Not covered: the research recorders in `scripts/` still name Yahoo hosts and need their own decision.

## No live trading figures in the repo (Nov 12, 2026, the owner: "rule and accept history")

**The rule.** No live trading figures of our real account go into this public repo or any branch: no per-market or per-strategy results, profit factors or win and loss counts of live trades, balances, sizing bases, savings, deposits, per-order or per-trade results, or early-sell counts. That covers docs, code comments, the changelog, test fixtures and research files. Rules, thresholds, caps and sizes in code (the $58.75 gold latch line, the 12% stop, the 5.5% window cap, fee schedules, the $200 politics budget), backtest and research results on public data, strategy names, dates and decisions are not live figures and stay. Say what a live result meant in plain words ("a small loss", "positive", "worse than its history") and keep the reasoning; put the number in a message to the owner or on the owner's private pages.

**History is accepted.** Past commits are not rewritten. The tracked files were cleaned in an ordinary pull request (live numbers replaced by neutral phrases or ""); older commits still hold the figures. **Desks, testers and workers must not commit live numbers on any branch**, including research branches and reports. `docs/RND_CHARTER.md` section 2 lists it as out of bounds. `test/noLiveFiguresGates.test.js` is a narrow gate for the common phrasings of a result ("profit factor 0.5 over 65 fills", "N settled, N won", "net +$N", a market name followed by a signed dollar amount); it cannot tell every threshold from every result, so the rule, not the gate, is what binds. A research or design line that trips it on purpose carries `[research]` or `[design]`. `docs/CHARTER.md` is owner-only and was not edited; the owner may want a line there. Test constants that mirror a real account value (for example the base in `test/kalshiGoldKillGates.test.js`) are reported to the owner and left alone.

## Gold keeps a shadow record after the latch trips (Nov 12, 2026, the owner: "Make sure gold keeps a shadow record after the latch trips")

Log only. `SHADOW_RULES` (`functions/kalshiShadowLib.js`) holds one rule again, **GOLDOFF**: gold (`KXGOLD15M`), the favourite side at 0.88 to 0.97, 330 to 400 seconds left, 1 contract, one row per window (`kalshiShadowRows/GOLDOFF_<ticker>`, written with `create`, so two instances during a deploy write one row). **Why:** once gold's kill latch trips, `runL1Tick` drops gold before it reads a quote, so no order and no row exist and the written return rule (shadow profit factor 1.3 or better over 60 trades and 7 days, second half 1.3, then a reviewed re-arm at half size for 30 trades) could never be met. It is the one written exception to the favourite-late shadow cap (`docs/STRATEGY_LIFECYCLE.md` 7c; pre-registration `kalshi-scalper/README.md#SHADOW-GOLDOFF`, with counterparty, prediction, kill criterion and `forwardReadDate` 2026-11-19, the earliest review: 7 days, or the 60th settled fillable row if that is later). It is gold's own measurement, not a candidate: no incubator slot, deleted in the edit that re-arms or abandons gold. **Does it write while the session is off? Yes, now.** The shadow tick used to sit inside the session-on branch of `kalshiLiveArmed`, so a loss stop or a stop by hand silenced it; it now runs whenever the minute function runs and no armed test order is on. With the session on it uses the quotes the tick already holds (no new request). With the session off it makes ONE public market read for gold, only in the minute or two around the entry window (`shadowSeriesDue`, 2 to 3 reads per 15 minutes), no key, no signing, no order, and settles closed rows every 5th minute (8 s budget). All of it is the LAST step of the minute, after the settle sweep, the account snapshot and the exit alarm, so it can delay none of them. With the registry empty none of this runs. The report's GOLDOFF row carries `returnRule` (met, rows, days, profit factor, second half) beside the unchanged lifecycle status. The Strategies page board deliberately does not list it (its bar is the candidate bar, not this one). **What it is not:** a shadow row is held to settlement at the touch with the 10 contract fee model and ignores quiet hours, so gold's 25c early sell is not modelled; it is a research figure, not the live cash profit factor. Rows start only from the deploy minute; earlier windows cannot be rebuilt. Gates: `test/goldOffShadowGates.test.js` GO1 to GO9 (GO6 proves every read, write, request and console line other than the shadow log is identical with the rule removed, with the session on, with gold latched and with the session off); `test/shadowLogGates.test.js` SH4 and SH5 and `test/strategyBoardGates.test.js` SB8 were changed on purpose. Needs `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh` (the minute function) and, for the report field, `bash functions/deploy-report.sh`.

## OICORE oiRise log (Nov 12, 2026, the owner approved the logging PR)

Logging only. A placed second-slot order stores `oiRise`, the open interest rise its signal fired on (from the public candles the tick already read; null when there is none). The nightly report lists it on slot order rows and extends the skip section: per skip `oiSeen` (the rise judged), `oiMin` (the strategy's line), `shortBy` (how far under it, oi_below only) and `result`, plus `openInterest` counts (`belowLine`, `nearMisses` within 10,000 contracts under the line, each split by result). Existing fields are unchanged. **The report makes no Kalshi call and holds no key**, so `result` comes only from settled shadow rows it already reads (`resultsFromRows`); a market with no such row has `result: null`, and a later reader joins the rows (ticker, oiRise, line) to public settled results itself. No order, size, threshold, rule or kill rule changed; nothing that decides an order reads the field. Needs `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh` (the order record) and `bash functions/deploy-report.sh` (the report). Tests `test/oicoreOiRiseLogGates.test.js` OR1 to OR7.

## Gold stays off until a shadow proves it (Nov 12, 2026, the owner)

Docs only (`docs/STRATEGY_LIFECYCLE.md` section 5b, `docs/GOLD_KILL_LATCH.md` "After the trip"). (1) If the gold kill latch trips, gold stays off with no automatic restart; it returns only after a shadow profit factor of 1.3 or better (60 trades, 7 days, second half 1.3 or better) and then a reviewed re-arm at half size for 30 trades. (2) While gold is off, OICORE and EVENT830 stay under their own kill rules, small; nothing new goes live until it earns it. Investigation finding: once gold is latched nothing logs its would-be orders (the tick drops it before quotes, the shadow registry is empty, the quote recorder covers only the last 150 seconds), so the return rule cannot yet be met. The recommended fix is one log-only shadow rule on gold; it is not built.

## Slow-family paper screen (Nov 12, 2026, the owner)

Docs only, in `docs/POLITICS_RULES.md` section 8. Families with fewer than about 52 independent events a year (the owner moved the line from 26 to 52 the same day, so weekly families qualify; monthly, weekly or seasonal: economics releases, court terms, elections, clinical) cannot reach the main research bar for years, so they get a separate, lower screen for a PAPER LIST only: cash profit factor 1.3 or better on at least 30 independent events, both halves positive, a pinned cutoff, read once. Below 30 events the label is "no evidence at n = x", never PASS. It permits no live order; a live probe still needs the owner's own popup with the exact spec, tiny, inside the $200 budget and the parlay limits, labelled unproven, and the favourite-and-hold ban stays except the combos exception. The lower bar lets false positives through: about 1 in 7 (roughly 15%) of rules with no edge pass at n = 30, so a listing means "survived a weak filter"; with 25 eligible cards the chance that at least one no-edge card is listed is about 98%. Re-screen: only three slow cards hold 30 events (the unemployment rounding card, elections against polls and ECONLAG); the rest read "no evidence at n = x". Pointers added in `docs/RND_CHARTER.md` section 6 and `docs/STRATEGY_LIFECYCLE.md` 7e.

## Perps recorders: starts packed into the windows (Nov 12, 2026)

Diagnosis: GitHub ran only 9 of the roughly 165 scheduled starts of `record-perps-fast.yml` in its first week (run list, Nov 6 to Nov 12), and none of the scheduled ones landed inside a window (windows are 09:10 to 10:30 and 15:40 to 17:00 New York, which is 14:10 to 15:30 and 20:40 to 22:00 UTC in winter), so the only file is a close that a manual start recorded on Nov 6. One run started at 22:00:03 UTC on Nov 9, three seconds after the close window ended. The script has no skip guard that fires when late (it records to the window end), so it is the dropped starts, not the script, and the old `*/20` cron spent 33 starts a day mostly outside the windows. The hourly recorder lost starts the same way (`7 * * * *`, commits at 02:49Z and 10:52Z with nothing between). Fix: the fast recorder's starts are packed into the windows (every 10 minutes from 13:02 to 15:22 UTC and 19:42 to 21:52 UTC, covering both daylight saving offsets, 29 starts a day against 33), under the same never-cancelling concurrency group; a later start queues behind a running one and finds the window recorded or over, and the existing file-exists check keeps it idempotent. The hourly recorder starts at minutes 7 and 37 and a freshness step skips the sample when the newest row is under 45 minutes old, so it is still at most 24 samples a day. Request rate (one plain GET every 30 seconds inside a window, one GET per hourly sample), stop dates, permissions (`contents: write`) and the push target (`perp-data` only) are unchanged. Runner minutes: the windows dominate (up to about 160 a day), the hourly recorder adds up to about 24 one-minute skipped runs a day, and the fast recorder's out-of-window starts fall by 4. The intended data is now actually recorded (the previous schedule recorded almost nothing); the rows per window are as designed. This takes real runner use for the 30 second recorder from near zero (9 of about 165 starts ran in a week and none landed in a window) to about 160 minutes a day on weekdays, roughly 3,500 a month, plus about 24 a day for skipped hourly starts, roughly 500 a month, against the Charter's roughly 250 a month flag (docs/RND_CHARTER.md). The repo is public so standard-runner minutes may be free; the owner accepts or lowers it. Gates R2 and PF5.

## Politics recorder: more starts, one poll each (Nov 12, 2026)

GitHub dropped most hourly starts of `record-politics.yml` (3 successful runs in 19 hours, none failed), so a dropped start cost two polls and about an hour of quotes. Now: starts at minutes 7, 22, 37 and 52 (still one never-cancelling concurrency group, so runs queue and never overlap), each run does ONE poll and ends (about 4 minutes), and a freshness step skips the poll when the newest `polrec` folder on `pol-data` is under 25 minutes old. So a full set of starts still gives about one poll per 30 minutes (at most 48 a day), and a dropped start is covered by the next one at 15 minutes later. Folder names are `YYYY-MM-DD/HHMM-<run id>-<attempt>`, so two runs never write the same file; the push keeps the six-try loop. The hourly results read moved to the first run of each hour (started before minute 20); a missed one is caught by the 48 hour lookback. Figures (an estimate from the series count, 42 series, about 45 requests a poll): up to 48 polls and about 24 results reads a day, so about 3,200 requests a day, about 2 a minute on average and never above 12 a minute (the 5 second gap is unchanged); about 400 runner minutes a day (it was about 840; free on a public repo); data roughly 1 MB a day gzipped, an estimate from the row width. Stop date 2027-02-28 and every guard unchanged. Gates PR3, PR4, PR10.

## Book read labelled as after the send; update stamps logged (Nov 12, 2026, the owner, log only)

The decision-time book snapshot (`bookYes5`, `bookNo5`, `bookReadMs`, since Nov 11) is read AFTER the order's POST was answered, in parallel with the rise log, never before: no book read is made before the send and none was added, so the order is not delayed. It is now labelled so nobody mistakes it for the book the order met: `bookReadWhen` is always `"after_send"` and `bookReadAfterSendMs` is the read's answer time minus the send time (`sentAtMs`). If the book answer carries top-level fields named like an update time they are kept as `bookUpdate` (at most 4 short scalars), and the same kind of field on the market object is kept as `seenUpdate` on the order record beside `seen`. Nothing that decides an order reads any of these; a failed, slow, empty or thrown read writes nothing and the order is byte-identical (gates PB1 to PB6, `test/presendBookLabelGates.test.js`). The nightly report rows carry `bookReadWhen` and `bookReadAfterSendMs` as compact values, no ids. Needs `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh` and `bash functions/deploy-report.sh`.

## Rolling shadow record in the nightly report (Nov 12, 2026)

`shadowRolling` (`functions/kalshiShadowLib.js`) adds a `rolling` object to each rule under `shadow.rules` in the nightly report; every existing field is unchanged and `shadowReport` itself is untouched. It is REPLAYED from the shadow rows on every read and never stored: the last 60 settled fillable signals up to the latest checkpoint (the 60th, 70th, 80th ... signal), with cash PF, first-half and second-half PF, days spanned, the largest day's share of net, and the count of the rarer outcome (wins or losses). Fewer than 60 fillable gives `status: "collecting"`, no flag and no figures. The flag is labelled `proposed, not a rule`: IN when PF is 1.3 or better on the window and its second half 1.2 or better, OUT when PF is under 1.0, between the two it keeps its last state, replayed checkpoint by checkpoint from the 60th, so reading the report more often or in a different row order cannot change it. The live-versus-shadow block is a documented null (`liveVersusShadow: null`): the shadow rows carry no link to live orders and joining them would need another read. Rule names and numbers only. Read only: no write, no Kalshi call, no key; nothing that decides an order reads it. Tests RR1 to RR6 in `test/shadowRollingReportGates.test.js`. Needs `bash functions/deploy-report.sh`.

Raw licensed data is in neither repo: it stays in a desk's scratch space. The rule against live trading figures in this public repo is unchanged.

The owner chose "treat it as personal use" (stocks would trade in the owner's own name for their own use, like the Kalshi account) and accepted the terms question as a risk. This is not a finding that the use is allowed. Registrations, scripts and try counts may be public. Spend so far: $0. A live use of any stock strategy still needs the owner's own popup.

## Long-hold paper log, phase 1 (Nov 12, 2026, the owner via the Head of R&D)

A general, log-only rule type for markets that are NOT 15 minute markets (`functions/kalshiLongholdLib.js`, `test/longholdPaperGates.test.js` LH1 to LH12, `docs/LONGHOLD_DESIGN.md`): an hours-before-close look window, a series list fixed in code, a contracts field (1 to 3), arms with price bands, a limit slip, hold to settlement, kill rules and a latch. **Nothing real is configured: `LONGHOLD_RULES` is empty**, so the deployed functions make no read and no write for it. The approval NO side was deliberately NOT made its first entry (its main arm is the favourite side held to settlement, the owner's banned shape); politics series are test fixtures only. The first configuration is a later reviewed edit with its registration in front of it. It is separate from `SHADOW_RULES` on purpose (a long-hold rule has a different shape, and an invalid entry there would refuse GOLDOFF's registry too). **No order is possible from it:** the library imports nothing and names no order, slot, sizing or signing code; its only network use is a public series list read (to choose, at most 1 a minute) and public single-market reads (to price, at most 3 a minute; settling at most 3 every 5th minute); rows are priced from the single-market read, never the list copy (up to about 11 seconds old); its only writes are `kalshiLongholdRows` and `kalshiLongholdControl/latch`; nothing that decides an order reads either. A latched arm stays off until a reviewed edit moves `rearmAt`. The nightly report gains a compact `longhold` section (no ids) only when a rule is registered. Phase 2 (a switched-off order path) is a design section only. Note `SLOT_ENABLED` in `kalshiSlotLib.js` reads `true` on main; this change does not touch it or any slot code. Needs `bash functions/deploy-sizing.sh` (the minute function; a no-op until a rule is registered) and `bash functions/deploy-report.sh` (the report section).
